1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	_VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL = "verify"
16	
17	
18	def is_verify_flow(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
19	    """Return True when the issue carries the ``verify`` label."""
20	    return _VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL in issue.get("labels", [])
21	
22	
23	def _shape_task_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
24	    """Shape-task preamble for verify-flow worker sessions.
25	
26	    Instructs the worker to check for an existing Task Shaper output
27	    block in the Linear issue body before invoking ``/shape:task``. The
28	    check-first approach handles both fresh sessions (block absent →
29	    invoke) and §14 resumes (block present → reuse).
30	    """
31	    return (
32	        f"Shaping step (run before implementing): fetch the Linear issue body "
33	        f"for {identifier} and check for a Task Shaper output block "
34	        "(delimited by `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-START -->` and `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-END -->`). "
35	        "If the block is present, use it as your implementation guide and skip "
36	        "re-running `/shape:task`. If the block is absent, run "
37	        f"`/shape:task {identifier}` first and use its output as your "
38	        "implementation guide.\n\n"
39	    )
40	
41	
42	_TAIL = (
43	    "before marking Done: review the working-tree changes for correctness "
44	    "and quality, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
45	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
46	)
47	
48	_STACK_TAIL = (
49	    "before marking Done: review the working-tree changes for correctness "
50	    "and quality, fix Critical/Required findings, commit to the issue branch "
51	    "without pushing, write .drain-handoff.json, then post a review-summary "
52	    "comment on the issue and transition to Done."
53	)
54	
55	
56	def _resume_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
57	    """Resume preamble for a worktree carrying prior committed work.
58	
59	    Inserted as the first line inside the preamble (after the ``---``
60	    separator, before "Execution instructions:") so the agent reads it
61	    ahead of the procedure but the tail still holds the last-line
62	    position the four-segment ordering reserves for it.
63	    """
64	    return (
65	        f"Resuming issue {identifier}: this worktree carries prior committed "
66	        "work from an earlier session that was halted. Run "
67	        "`git log --oneline main..HEAD` and `git status` first to read what "
68	        "is already done, then continue from that point — do not restart "
69	        "from scratch.\n\n"
70	    )
71	
72	
73	def _normal_preamble(
74	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
75	) -> str:
76	    return (
77	        "---\n\n"
78	        f"{resume_segment}"
79	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
80	        "Execution instructions:\n"
81	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
82	        "- Base branch: main\n"
83	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
84	        "before marking Done):\n"
85	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
86	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
87	        "are at your discretion.\n"
88	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
89	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
90	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
91	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
92	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
93	        '(state: "Done").\n'
94	    )
95	
96	
97	def _stack_preamble(
98	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
99	) -> str:
100	    return (
101	        "---\n\n"
102	        f"{resume_segment}"
103	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
104	        "Execution instructions:\n"
105	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
106	        "- Base branch: main\n"
107	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
108	        "before marking Done):\n"
109	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
110	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
111	        "are at your discretion.\n"
112	        "  3. Commit to the issue branch (do not push).\n"
113	        "  4. Write `.drain-handoff.json` in the worktree root with keys:\n"
114	        "     - `pr_title`: a concise PR title (≤ 70 characters)\n"
115	        "     - `pr_body`: markdown with ## What, ## Why, and ## What to review "
116	        "sections\n"
117	        '     - `findings`: `{"critical": N, "required": N}` counts from the '
118	        "review\n"
119	        "  5. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
120	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
121	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
122	        "  6. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
123	        '(state: "Done").\n'
124	    )
125	
126	
127	def build(
128	    issue: dict[str, Any],
129	    worktree: Path,
130	    *,
131	    resumed: bool = False,
132	    stack: bool = False,
133	) -> str:
134	    title = issue.get("title", "")
135	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
136	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
137	
138	    resume_segment = _resume_directive(identifier) if resumed else ""
139	    shape_task_segment = _shape_task_directive(identifier) if is_verify_flow(issue) else ""
140	
141	    if stack:
142	        preamble = _stack_preamble(identifier, worktree, resume_segment, shape_task_segment)
143	        tail = _STACK_TAIL
144	    else:
145	        preamble = _normal_preamble(identifier, worktree, resume_segment, shape_task_segment)
146	        tail = _TAIL
147	
148	    return (
149	        f"# {title}\n\n"
150	        f"{description}\n\n"
151	        f"{preamble}\n"
152	        f"{tail}\n"
153	    )
154

> AGENT

Here's what happened.

## Root cause: a half-finished refactor left a gap where the PR used to be opened

ABA-386 ran in **stack mode** and completed cleanly — but nothing in the current code actually opens a PR anymore.

**What the run did** (from the runlog `793f…120327`):
- `exit_code: 0`, `final_linear_state: "Done"` — the worker finished its whole completion sequence
- Committed to a local branch: `ABA-386` → `f52f140 feat: crash-proof watch-pane formatter filter`
- Worktree already cleaned up, branch **never pushed**, **no PR**, **no remote branch**

**Why no PR got opened:**

Because `drain-cycle` isn't in `push_to_main_repos` (that key is unset in `~/.drain-cycle/repos.yml`), `orchestrator.py:528` set `stack = True`. In stack mode the worker prompt (`_stack_preamble` in `prompt.py`) tells the agent to:

> commit to the issue branch (do not push) → write `.drain-handoff.json` → comment → mark Done

It is explicitly told **not** to push, and it's never told to open a PR. The handoff file was the contract: the orchestrator used to read it, assemble the branch into a Graphite stack, submit the PR, and post the link back to Linear.

**That orchestrator path was deleted earlier today** — commit `86334a9` "feat: remove orchestrator graphite/PR-assembly path (ABA-384) (#14)", merged ~21:59, on the rationale that *"the skill (N08) now owns stacked-PR submission."* Your run was at 12:03 the next entry — after that merge.

So the two halves don't meet:
- The orchestrator no longer assembles/submits PRs (removed in #14)
- The worker prompt still says "commit, write handoff, **don't push**" and never invokes a skill to submit the PR

The handoff JSON is now written into a void — nothing consumes it. The branch sits locally, the issue gets marked Done, and no PR is ever opened.

## To recover ABA-386 right now

The work isn't lost — it's on the local `ABA-386` branch. Want me to push it and open the PR manually?

```
git push -u origin ABA-386 && gh pr create --head ABA-386 ...
```

## The actual bug to fix

The stack-mode worker prompt needs to either (a) drive the stacked-PR submission itself via the skill that #14 assumed owns it, or (b) the orchestrator's PR-assembly removal was premature. Right now neither side opens the PR. This is worth its own Linear issue — the regression was introduced by ABA-384 without updating `prompt.py`'s `_stack_preamble`/`_STACK_TAIL` to actually trigger submission.

Want me to push ABA-386's branch + open its PR, file the regression issue, or both?

1	"""Stop-hook guard for spawned drain workers.
2	
3	A ``claude -p`` session that loads a "report findings / await input" skill
4	can end its turn before the completion sequence (commit + handoff + Linear
5	comment + Done) runs. The session exits cleanly with the implementation
6	green but uncommitted, and the orchestrator only sees a generic not-Done
7	halt with no signal that the work was finished.
8	
9	This module is the Stop hook the worker session runs at end-of-turn. It
10	fires only when the orchestrator has planted ``.drain-guard.json`` in the
11	worktree root, so interactive sessions and non-drain runs are a silent
12	no-op. When the marker is present and the session is trying to stop with
13	work still uncaptured (dirty tree or, in stack mode, no
14	``.drain-handoff.json``), it returns ``decision: block`` to push the
15	agent through the completion sequence. After ``max_blocks`` re-injections
16	it gives up, writes ``.drain-guard-tripped`` with the observed state, and
17	lets the session exit — the orchestrator reads that marker and tags the
18	halt as ``worker_stopped_incomplete`` rather than the generic not-Done
19	line.
20	"""
21	from __future__ import annotations
22	
23	import json
24	import subprocess
25	import sys
26	from dataclasses import dataclass
27	from pathlib import Path
28	from typing import Literal, TextIO
29	
30	from .handoff import HANDOFF_FILE, read as _read_handoff
31	
32	MARKER_FILE = ".drain-guard.json"
33	TRIPPED_FILE = ".drain-guard-tripped"
34	DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS = 3
35	TRIPPED_HALT_REASON = "worker_stopped_incomplete"
36	
37	
38	@dataclass(frozen=True)
39	class GuardConfig:
40	    """State written by the orchestrator and updated by the hook.
41	
42	    ``mode`` is ``"stack"`` (handoff-driven assembly) or ``"push"`` (direct
43	    push to main). ``count`` is the number of times this hook has already
44	    returned ``block`` for the session — incremented in place. ``max_blocks``
45	    is the cap before the guard gives up.
46	    """
47	
48	    mode: str
49	    count: int
50	    max_blocks: int
51	
52	
53	def write_marker(
54	    worktree: Path,
55	    *,
56	    mode: Literal["stack", "push"],
57	    max_blocks: int = DEFAULT_MAX_BLOCKS,
58	) -> None:
59	    """Plant the per-session marker the hook keys on.
60	
61	    Called by the orchestrator immediately before spawning a worker. Replaces
62	    any prior marker so a resumed worktree starts fresh from ``count=0``.
63	    Also clears any stale tripped-marker from a previous attempt so the
64	    orchestrator's post-session read isn't a false positive.
65	    """
66	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
67	        json.dumps({"mode": mode, "count": 0, "max_blocks": max_blocks})
68	    )
69	    tripped = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
70	    if tripped.exists():
71	        tripped.unlink()
72	
73	
74	def read_marker(worktree: Path) -> GuardConfig | None:
75	    """Return the guard state for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent/invalid."""
76	    path = worktree / MARKER_FILE
77	    try:
78	        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
79	    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
80	        return None
81	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
82	        return None
83	    mode = payload.get("mode")
84	    count = payload.get("count")
85	    max_blocks = payload.get("max_blocks")
86	    if mode not in ("stack", "push"):
87	        return None
88	    if not isinstance(count, int) or not isinstance(max_blocks, int):
89	        return None
90	    return GuardConfig(mode=mode, count=count, max_blocks=max_blocks)
91	
92	
93	def _write_marker_count(worktree: Path, config: GuardConfig, *, count: int) -> None:
94	    (worktree / MARKER_FILE).write_text(
95	        json.dumps({"mode": config.mode, "count": count, "max_blocks": config.max_blocks})
96	    )
97	
98	
99	def read_tripped(worktree: Path) -> str | None:
100	    """Return the tripped reason for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if not tripped."""
101	    path = worktree / TRIPPED_FILE
102	    try:
103	        return path.read_text().strip() or None
104	    except OSError:
105	        return None
106	
107	
108	# drain-cycle's own per-session artefacts live at the worktree root and are
109	# expected to be untracked. ``_git_dirty`` filters them out so the guard
110	# doesn't see itself (or the handoff file written *after* the commit) as
111	# uncaptured work.
112	_OWN_ARTEFACTS = frozenset({MARKER_FILE, TRIPPED_FILE, HANDOFF_FILE})
113	
114	
115	def _git_dirty(worktree: Path) -> bool:
116	    """Return True if the worktree has uncommitted changes (excluding our own artefacts).
117	
118	    ``git status --porcelain`` lines are ``XY <path>`` (two status chars,
119	    a space, then the path). Untracked entries for our own artefacts —
120	    ``.drain-guard.json``, ``.drain-guard-tripped``, ``.drain-handoff.json``
121	    — are skipped: the handoff is written after the commit by design, and
122	    the guard's own markers are not the agent's work.
123	    """
124	    result = subprocess.run(
125	        ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
126	        cwd=str(worktree),
127	        check=False,
128	        capture_output=True,
129	        text=True,
130	    )
131	    if result.returncode != 0:
132	        return False
133	    for line in result.stdout.splitlines():
134	        if len(line) < 4:
135	            continue
136	        path = line[3:].strip()
137	        if path in _OWN_ARTEFACTS:
138	            continue
139	        return True
140	    return False
141	
142	
143	def _has_handoff(worktree: Path) -> bool:
144	    return _read_handoff(worktree) is not None
145	
146	
147	@dataclass(frozen=True)
148	class Decision:
149	    """The hook's response to one Stop event.
150	
151	    ``block_reason`` is non-None when the agent should be re-prompted (the
152	    hook emits ``{"decision": "block", "reason": ...}``). ``tripped_reason``
153	    is non-None when the guard has given up and written ``TRIPPED_FILE`` —
154	    the hook then exits silently and the orchestrator surfaces the tripped
155	    state in the halt line.
156	    """
157	
158	    block_reason: str | None
159	    tripped_reason: str | None
160	
161	
162	_BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK = (
163	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
164	    "uncommitted changes or no .drain-handoff.json yet. Complete the "
165	    "remaining steps now: commit any pending changes to the issue branch "
166	    "(do not push), write .drain-handoff.json (pr_title, pr_body with "
167	    "## What / ## Why / ## What to review, findings: {critical, required}), "
168	    "post a review-summary comment on the Linear issue, then transition the "
169	    "issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, leave the issue In Progress "
170	    "and post a comment naming the blocker — do not stop silently."
171	)
172	
173	_BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH = (
174	    "drain-cycle stop-guard: the issue is not finished — the worktree has "
175	    "uncommitted changes. Complete the remaining steps now: commit and push "
176	    "to main, post a review-summary comment on the Linear issue, then "
177	    "transition the issue to Done. If you are genuinely blocked, leave the "
178	    "issue In Progress and post a comment naming the blocker — do not stop "
179	    "silently."
180	)
181	
182	
183	def evaluate(worktree: Path, *, stop_hook_active: bool) -> Decision:
184	    """Decide whether to block the Stop event, give up, or pass through.
185	
186	    The decision tree, in order:
187	
188	    * No marker → not a drain worker session. Pass through.
189	    * Stack mode + clean tree + valid handoff → completion sequence ran.
190	      Pass through.
191	    * Push mode + clean tree → completion sequence ran. Pass through.
192	    * Otherwise: incomplete. If ``count < max_blocks`` (and we're not
193	      already deep in a block-loop per Claude's ``stop_hook_active`` flag),
194	      increment and emit a block. If at the cap, write the tripped file
195	      and pass through so the session can exit cleanly.
196	
197	    ``stop_hook_active`` mirrors Claude's payload — when True the hook is
198	    being re-invoked from within a previous block, so the cap applies even
199	    if the count somehow lagged.
200	    """
201	    config = read_marker(worktree)
202	    if config is None:
203	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
204	
205	    dirty = _git_dirty(worktree)
206	    handoff_ok = _has_handoff(worktree) if config.mode == "stack" else True
207	    complete = (not dirty) and handoff_ok
208	    if complete:
209	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=None)
210	
211	    state_summary = _describe_incomplete(config.mode, dirty=dirty, handoff_ok=handoff_ok)
212	
213	    if config.count >= config.max_blocks or (stop_hook_active and config.count > 0):
214	        reason = f"{state_summary} after {config.count} re-injection(s)"
215	        (worktree / TRIPPED_FILE).write_text(reason)
216	        return Decision(block_reason=None, tripped_reason=reason)
217	
218	    _write_marker_count(worktree, config, count=config.count + 1)
219	    prompt = _BLOCK_PROMPT_STACK if config.mode == "stack" else _BLOCK_PROMPT_PUSH
220	    return Decision(block_reason=prompt, tripped_reason=None)
221	
222	
223	def _describe_incomplete(mode: str, *, dirty: bool, handoff_ok: bool) -> str:
224	    parts = []
225	    if dirty:
226	        parts.append("uncommitted changes in worktree")
227	    if mode == "stack" and not handoff_ok:
228	        parts.append("no valid .drain-handoff.json")
229	    return ", ".join(parts) or "incomplete state"
230	
231	
232	def run(stdin: TextIO, stdout: TextIO, *, cwd: Path | None = None) -> int:
233	    """Read the Stop hook payload from ``stdin`` and emit a decision on ``stdout``.
234	
235	    Exit code 0 always; the decision is carried in the JSON body. Errors
236	    parsing the payload fall through to a silent pass — a broken hook must
237	    not block a legitimate stop.
238	    """
239	    try:
240	        payload = json.load(stdin)
241	    except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
242	        payload = {}
243	    stop_hook_active = bool(payload.get("stop_hook_active"))
244	    worktree = cwd if cwd is not None else Path(payload.get("cwd") or ".").resolve()
245	
246	    decision = evaluate(worktree, stop_hook_active=stop_hook_active)
247	    if decision.block_reason is not None:
248	        json.dump({"decision": "block", "reason": decision.block_reason}, stdout)
249	    return 0
250	
251	
252	def main() -> None:
253	    sys.exit(run(sys.stdin, sys.stdout))
254	
```

> AGENT

Confirmed. Commit `86334a9` (ABA-384, #14) introduced **two** regressions, and the stop-hook that just fired at me is the second one in action.

## Bug 2: `.drain-guard.json` was accidentally committed into the repo

`.drain-guard.json` is, by design, a **per-session, untracked** marker the orchestrator plants *inside a worktree* right before spawning a worker (`write_marker`, `stop_guard.py:53`). The module docstring is explicit: it "fires only when the orchestrator has planted `.drain-guard.json` in the worktree root, so interactive sessions and non-drain runs are a silent no-op" (lines 11-12), and the file is "expected to be untracked" (line 108).

But commit #14 ran `git add .drain-guard.json` and committed it into the **main repo root** with `{"mode": "stack", ...}`. Consequences:

- Every interactive `claude` session in this repo now runs the Stop hook, `read_marker` finds the committed marker, sees `mode=stack` + no `.drain-handoff.json` → `complete=False` → **blocks the session**. That's the message you just saw fired at me.
- The hook already mutated it this session: `count` went `0 → 1` and it now shows as `M` (an uncommitted change to a tracked file that shouldn't exist).

## Summary — both from the same commit

| | Bug | Cause |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | No PR opened for ABA-386 | Orchestrator PR-assembly deleted; stack worker prompt never updated to submit the PR itself — handoff written into a void |
| **2** | Stop-guard blocks every session | The per-session untracked marker got committed into the repo root |

That commit landed ~12 min before your ABA-386 run, so it's the first drain to hit both.

## Fixes I'd apply

**Bug 2 (quick, do now):**
```
git rm --cached .drain-guard.json
echo ".drain-guard.json" >> .gitignore   # also .drain-guard-tripped, .drain-handoff.json
```
This stops the misfire immediately. The three worktree artefacts (`.drain-guard.json`, `.drain-guard-tripped`, `.drain-handoff.json`) should all be gitignored so they can never be committed again.

**Bug 1 (needs a decision):** the stack-mode `_stack_preamble` in `prompt.py` needs to actually drive PR submission — either invoke the skill that #14 assumed "now owns stacked-PR submission" (`shape:pr-finishing` / `shape:pr-prepare` exist in the skill list), or the orchestrator removal was premature and should be partly reverted.

Want me to:
1. Apply the Bug 2 fix + push ABA-386's branch and open its PR manually (recover the lost work), and
2. File a Linear issue for Bug 1 (the prompt/orchestrator gap)?

Both bugs are real regressions worth their own tickets. How do you want to proceed?

=== handoff.py ===
"""Handoff artefact written by a stack-mode agent for the assembler.

The spawned agent writes ``.drain-handoff.json`` at the worktree root before
finishing so the orchestrator's assembly step can turn it into a Graphite PR
without re-reading the agent's output. ``read`` never raises: a missing or
malformed file returns ``None`` so callers can treat it as "not ready yet."
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

HANDOFF_FILE = ".drain-handoff.json"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class HandoffData:
    pr_title: str
    pr_body: str
    findings: dict[str, int]


def write(worktree: Path, data: HandoffData) -> None:
    """Serialise ``data`` to ``<worktree>/.drain-handoff.json``."""
    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
    path.write_text(
        json.dumps(
            {
                "pr_title": data.pr_title,
                "pr_body": data.pr_body,
                "findings": data.findings,
            },
            indent=2,
        )
    )


def read(worktree: Path) -> HandoffData | None:
    """Return the handoff data for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent or invalid."""
    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE
    try:
        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):
        return None
    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
        return None
    pr_title = payload.get("pr_title")
    pr_body = payload.get("pr_body")
    findings = payload.get("findings")
    if not isinstance(pr_title, str) or not isinstance(pr_body, str):
        return None
    if not isinstance(findings, dict):
        return None
    return HandoffData(pr_title=pr_title, pr_body=pr_body, findings=findings)

> AGENT

Answers logged. Three of four are decisive; the fourth (the handoff contract) is genuinely the crux, and your two choices — **worker drives submission via skill** + **true cross-issue stacking is the point** — are in direct tension. Resolving that tension *is* what the handoff file should become. Here's my read.

## The tension you just created (on purpose, I think)

True Graphite stacking means PR N is branched on PR N-1's branch, in dependency order: `main ← 386 ← 387 ← 388…`. But you also want each **worker to own its own `gt`/`gh`**, and each worker runs isolated in its own worktree. Commit #14 removed `last_branch_per_repo` — so today every worktree branches from `main` independently. **An isolated worker has no idea what the previous issue's branch was.** Worker-driven submission and a real stack cannot both be true unless *something* carries the parent-branch baton between issues.

That "something" is the only honest job left for the handoff file.

## What I think should happen to the contract

**Repurpose it as a thin inter-issue baton — not a revival of the brittle assembly.** This is what reconciles all four of your answers:

- The **worker owns all `gt`/`gh`** (fixes the brittleness that drove #14 — the orchestrator never touches Graphite again).
- The **orchestrator owns only the cheap, robust part**: sequencing issues per repo, telling worker N+1 *"your base branch is `<worker N's branch>`"*, and posting the Linear PR-link comment.
- `.drain-handoff.json` shrinks from *"PR title/body/findings for the assembler to act on"* to *"here is my branch name and my PR URL"* — the output a worker hands back so the orchestrator can (a) seed the next worker's base and (b) comment the link to Linear.

So the data flow becomes:

```
orchestrator: base = main (or prev issue's branch from its handoff)
   → worker N prompt: "Base branch: <base>"
   → worker N: implement, commit, run pr-finishing skill (gt track --parent <base>, gt submit)
   → worker N: write .drain-handoff.json { branch, pr_url }
orchestrator: read handoff → post PR link to Linear → base := branch → spawn worker N+1
```

This is the **hybrid** option from Q2 in disguise — except the split is drawn so the orchestrator keeps *zero* git plumbing. It just passes a string forward and reads a string back. That's the part that was never brittle.

## The two things this forces that aren't free

1. **`Base branch: main` is now a lie in stack mode.** `_stack_preamble` (`prompt.py:107`) hardcodes `main`; the orchestrator must thread the real parent branch in, which means restoring a `last_branch_per_repo`-shaped piece of state — the *value*, not the gt machinery #14 deleted.
2. **Failure breaks the chain, and #14 also deleted the revert-from-Done halt** (`a3705d1` was removed). If worker 388 fails to submit, 389/390 have no valid base. You need a halt-and-stop semantic again: a worker that can't submit leaves the issue In Progress and the orchestrator stops the per-repo chain rather than stacking onto a phantom branch.

## My recommended direction (one line)

Worker owns `gt`/`gh` via a pr-finishing skill; orchestrator is reduced to a sequencer that threads parent-branch in and reads branch+PR-URL out of a slimmed `.drain-handoff.json`; restore a chain-halt on submission failure. **Don't delete `handoff.py` — slim it.** `CLAUDE.md`'s workflow section needs rewriting to match (it currently describes the deleted assembly path).

Two things I want to confirm before I write this up as a spec / Linear issues:

- Does `shape:pr-finishing` actually take a `--parent`-style base and run `gt track`/`gt submit`? Its description says it splits *one branch into per-commit-slice PRs* — that's a different axis from cross-issue stacking, and the spec depends on whether one skill covers both or we need the orchestrator to set the base before the skill runs. Want me to read the skill to confirm its real interface?
- Should I capture this as a `docs/ideas/` one-pager, or go straight to Linear issues (stacked, naturally — slim-handoff → worker-skill-submit → orchestrator-sequencer → chain-halt → docs) in the current cycle?

===== pr-finishing SKILL.md =====
---
name: pr-finishing
description: Finish an already-green, sliced development branch by submitting one small PR per commit slice. Use when work is complete, committed in reviewable slices, and ready for PR submission through Graphite or plain git.
---

## Purpose

`pr-finishing` turns an already-green feature branch into a stack of small PRs, one per commit slice. Prefer Graphite when available and configured; otherwise use plain git. Preserve the same PR body format on both paths.

## Use when

- Work is complete and tests pass.
- Changes are committed as independently reviewable slices, one logical change per commit.
- The operator asks to finish, submit, create stacked PRs, or submit the stack.
- Optional: a drain-cycle handoff file exists at `.drain-handoff.json`.
- Optional: a Linear issue ID is available for the final trail comment.

Do not use this skill to hide failing tests, submit a known-broken branch, or re-slice a squashed multi-change commit after the fact.

## Inputs

- Feature branch with one logical change per commit.
- Optional `.drain-handoff.json` containing an `issue` and `slices`.
- Optional Linear issue ID from the branch name, handoff file, user request, or available Linear plugin.
- Optional Graphite CLI, `gt`.
- GitHub CLI, `gh`, for PR creation and verification.

Example `.drain-handoff.json`:

```json
{
  "issue": "ABC-123",
  "slices": [
    {
      "sha": "<40-char SHA>",
      "title": "<PR title>",
      "why": "<optional motivation>"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Outputs

- One PR per slice.
- Each PR body uses the same What / Why / Focus template.
- If .drain-handoff.json exists, update it with pr_urls.
- If Linear is available and an issue ID is known, post a review-summary comment.
- If Linear is unavailable, include the review summary in the final response instead.

### 1. Restate the issue

Start with one sentence:

> This branch closes <issue> by delivering <summary>.

If no issue ID is known, derive the summary from the branch name and commit subjects.

### 2. Verify green state

Run the repo's required tests before creating any PRs.

If tests fail, stop. Report the failing command and relevant output. Do not submit PRs.

### 3. Determine slices

If .drain-handoff.json exists, read its slices array.

If it does not exist, derive slices from git:

`git log main..HEAD --reverse --format=%H%x00%B%x00%x00`

Use each commit as one slice. The first line of the commit message is the title; the remaining body is the Why.

If multiple unrelated changes are squashed into one commit, warn that the branch was not sliced during build. Continue only if it appears to be a single logical change, or the operator explicitly accepts the risk.

### 4. Choose submission path

Check Graphite preconditions in order:

`which gt gt auth status gt repo init --check`

Use these rules:

- If all checks pass, use the Graphite path.
- If gt is absent, use the plain-git path.
- If gt exists but auth or repo initialization fails, stop and show the exact error. Do not silently fall back to git.

### 5. Graphite path

For each slice, oldest first:

- Create or track a Graphite branch at that exact commit.
- Ensure the branch points to the slice commit, not the stack tip.
- Draft the PR body using the template below.

Submit the stack:

`gt stack submit --no-interactive`

After submission, set or correct each PR body with gh pr edit.

Wrap each gt call in a bounded recovery loop:

- On success: continue.
- On stale parent or out-of-sync error: run gt repo sync, retry once.
- On dirty working tree: stash, retry once, then restore the stash.
- On auth or repo initialization error: stop immediately.
- On any second failure: stop and report the exact error.

### 6. Plain-git path

Use this only when gt is absent.

For each slice, oldest first:

- Create a branch from the slice parent named <issue-or-branch>/<slug>-<n>.
- Cherry-pick the slice commit.
- Create the PR using the shared body template.
- Push the branch and create the PR with gh pr create.

The plain-git path must produce the same PR body and final trail as the Graphite path.

### 7. Verify PRs

Before posting any final trail, verify every PR URL is reachable:

`gh pr view <url>`

If any PR is missing or unreachable, stop and report the problem. Do not post the Linear comment yet.

If .drain-handoff.json exists, update it with pr_urls:

`{ "pr_urls": [ { "title": "<PR title>", "url": "<https://...>" } ] }`

### 8. Post final trail

If Linear is available and an issue ID is known, post one review-summary comment to the issue.

If Linear is unavailable or no issue ID is known, include the same summary in the final response.

If repo policy does not permit PRs, follow the repo policy and note the deviation in the summary.

## PR body template

Use this exact structure for every PR:

`## What <One to three sentences describing what this slice changes. Keep this diff-level, not ticket-level.> ## Why <Why this slice exists, what issue it supports, and why it can be reviewed independently. For later PRs, reference the prior PR when useful.> ## Focus <Specific files, decisions, or patterns to review. If there are no critical areas, write: "No critical review areas - safe for auto-merge.">`

## Review-summary template

`PR stack submitted: <n> PR(s) <slice-1-title>: <url> <slice-2-title>: <url> Path: graphite | plain-git Pre-submission review findings: critical: N, required: N (fixed: N, deferred: N)`

## Exit criteria

The skill is complete only when:

1. Tests passed before PR creation.
2. Each slice has exactly one PR.
3. Each PR body contains What, Why, and Focus.
```

# Slice-During-Build PR Finishing

## Problem Statement
**How might we** let each completed task land as a set of small, well-described, stacked
PRs — without the orchestrator owning fragile `gt`/`gh` invocations that halt the whole run
on any error?

## Recommended Direction
**Direction B: slice-during-build + a recovery-aware finishing skill.**

The root cause is that Linear issues are too coarse — one issue legitimately contains 3–5
independent changes. Two failed/rejected ways to fix this:
- **Pre-shaping** (shape:* skills cutting PRs at *planning* time) — too hard; you can't predict
  the right PR cuts before building.
- **Post-hoc re-slicing** (split the finished flat diff) — arguably harder; reconstructing
  independently-green commit boundaries from a squashed diff is error-prone.

B hits the sweet spot: **decide PR boundaries during the build, when the agent has the most
context and each slice is naturally green.** The worker commits in reviewable slices (one
logical change per commit). A final finishing skill then maps commits → a stacked set of PRs,
fills a What/Why/Focus body per PR, drives `gt` itself inside a **recovery loop** (read stderr,
re-track, rebase, detect missing `gt auth`/`gt init` preconditions), and only emits a halt
signal when genuinely stuck. `graphite.py` in the orchestrator shrinks toward zero — the agent,
not Python, holds the graphite knife, with a recovery loop between the commands and the halt.

This keeps your "correctness > throughput" stance: real, unrecoverable failures still stop the
line; transient gt friction no longer does.

## Relationship to ABA-366 (N08)
ABA-366 covers ~40% of this: it moves PR finishing into a skill, nails the What/Why/Focus body,
and posts the Linear trail. It does **not** cover (a) decomposing one coarse issue into multiple
small PRs — its "stacked" means stacked across issues, not splitting one issue; (b) slice-during-
build; (c) the retry-then-halt recovery loop (its resilience story is a git fallback for
gt-less machines, which is unrequested scope). N08 should either be expanded or paired with a
decomposition sibling, or you'll ship better-described fat PRs.

## Key Assumptions to Validate
- [ ] Worker-time commit discipline is reliable — *test:* run a real multi-change issue and check
      the worker produces clean, independently-green slices without heavy prompting.
- [ ] An agent can drive `gt track`/`gt submit` in a recovery loop more robustly than Python —
      *test:* inject a stale-parent / dirty-tree failure and confirm the skill recovers.
- [ ] Each slice builds + passes tests on its own (reviewer can review PR-by-PR) — *test:*
      `gt submit` each slice and run the suite at each stack level.
- [ ] Missing `gt auth`/`gt init` preconditions can be detected and reported clearly rather than
      surfacing as a cryptic submit abort — *test:* run in a repo without `gt init`.

## MVP Scope
**In:** worker prompt + handoff schema updated to commit in reviewable slices; a finishing skill
that maps slices → stacked PRs with What/Why/Focus bodies; a recovery loop around gt/gh that
halts only when unrecoverable; review-summary comment + Linear status (port from N08).
**Out:** plain-git fallback for gt-less environments; auto-`gt auth`; pre-shaping at planning time.

## Not Doing (and Why)
- **Pre-shaping PR cuts at planning time** — proven too hard; boundaries belong at build time.
- **Post-hoc re-slicing of a flat diff** — harder than slicing during build; only a fallback.
- **Keeping gt in orchestrator Python (harden-in-place)** — doesn't address fat PRs at all; you
  ruled out this owner.
- **Plain-git fallback (N08's gt-less path)** — unrequested scope; single-user, gt is present.

## Open Questions
- Does the worker commit-in-slices discipline survive `--dangerously-skip-permissions` without a
  human nudging boundaries? If not, the post-hoc fallback (A) becomes load-bearing.
- Should the finishing skill be one skill that supersedes/absorbs ABA-366, or a decomposition
  skill that runs *before* N08's body+trail step?

> AGENT

I now have everything I need to decide. Two findings change the shape of the fix — both resolved without you:

**Finding A — the skill already does almost all of it.** `shape:pr-finishing` (gt 1.8.6 is installed) already: derives slices from `git log main..HEAD`, runs `gt stack submit`, writes `pr_urls` back to `.drain-handoff.json`, and posts the Linear review-summary comment. The worker just needs to *invoke* it.

**Finding B — the two handoff schemas already diverged.** drain-cycle's `handoff.py` writes `{pr_title, pr_body, findings}`. The skill expects `{issue, slices}` *in* and writes `{pr_urls}` *out*, and treats the file as **optional** (it derives slices from git if absent). So the worker doesn't need to hand-author a PR body at all — the skill generates What/Why/Focus per slice.

And the branch name is deterministic (`ABA-387` = the worktree branch), so the orchestrator never needed the handoff to learn it.

## Questions I answered myself (no input needed)

| # | Question | Answer |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Which skill submits? | `shape:pr-finishing` — exists, gt-first, posts Linear comment, writes `pr_urls` |
| 2 | Does the skill take a parent/base? | **No** — it slices `main..HEAD`. This is the *only* blocker to true chaining |
| 3 | Is `handoff.read` orphaned? | Yes — zero callers since #14 |
| 4 | Is gt available? | Yes, 1.8.6, on PATH |
| 5 | Can the orchestrator thread a base branch? | Yes — `worktree.ensure(repo, id, base=…)` already takes it |
| 6 | How are issues ordered? | `plan.order`, processed sequentially — chaining baton fits naturally |
| 7 | Who posts the Linear PR comment? | The skill (worker-owned). Orchestrator stops posting it |
| 8 | What does the slim handoff carry? | Just `pr_urls` (skill's output) — orchestrator reads it to confirm submission + runlog |
| 9 | Failure handling? | #14 deleted the chain-halt; must restore: no `pr_urls` ⇒ leave In Progress, halt the per-repo chain |

## The one real fork (I'm choosing a default, not asking)

True cross-issue chaining (`386←387←388`) needs **two** things: the orchestrator basing worktree N+1 on branch N, **and** the skill slicing `<base>..HEAD` instead of `main..HEAD` (a cross-repo change to `agent-skills-shaper`). The regression — "no PR at all" — needs *neither*. So I'm splitting the build:

- **Increment 1 (now, drain-cycle only):** worker invokes `pr-finishing` → each issue lands its own PR (or intra-issue slice-stack) off main. Fixes the regression completely. Slim `handoff.py` to `pr_urls`, restore the halt, rewrite `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Increment 2 (next, two repos):** orchestrator base-branch baton + teach `pr-finishing` a `base` param → real `386←387` chain.

This ships a working PR on the very next drain while the harder chaining lands behind it. Let me wire Increment 1 now — reading the orchestrator's post-worker section first.

> AGENT

The teardown logic seals it: once the worker marks Done, the orchestrator **removes the worktree** — so PR submission *must* happen inside the worker session, before Done, while gt/gh can still run in the worktree. That's exactly what invoking the skill achieves. And the stop-guard already gates stopping on a valid handoff — which means once I change the handoff schema to the skill's `pr_urls`, the guard automatically enforces "PR actually submitted." One more reason the pieces fit.

Here's the Increment 1 build, then I'll execute it:

**Increment 1 — touch list (drain-cycle only):**
1. `handoff.py` — schema `{pr_title,pr_body,findings}` → `{pr_urls:[{title,url}]}` (the skill's output). `read()` validates `pr_urls`. This makes the stop-guard gate on *real submission*.
2. `prompt.py` — `_stack_preamble`/`_STACK_TAIL`: replace "commit, hand-author handoff, don't push" with "commit in reviewable slices → review → invoke `/shape:pr-finishing` (submits the stack, writes `pr_urls`, posts the Linear comment) → mark Done."
3. `stop_guard.py` — docstring update (logic already routes through `handoff.read`, so it's automatically correct).
4. `CLAUDE.md` — rewrite the drain-mode completion sequence to match.
5. Tests for prompt + handoff.

=== handoff test ===
"""Tests for ``drain_cycle.handoff``.

``read`` must never raise: a missing file, a truncated JSON write, or a
structurally wrong payload all return ``None``. A well-formed file returns
a typed ``HandoffData`` object with the expected field values.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

from drain_cycle.handoff import HANDOFF_FILE, HandoffData, read, write


def _valid_data() -> HandoffData:
    return HandoffData(
        pr_title="feat: add stack-ready worktrees",
        pr_body="## What\nAdds stacking.\n\n## Why\nNeeded for Graphite.\n\n## What to review\nworktree.py",
        findings={"critical": 0, "required": 1},
    )


def test_write_then_read_round_trips(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    data = _valid_data()
    write(tmp_path, data)
    result = read(tmp_path)

    assert result is not None
    assert result.pr_title == data.pr_title
    assert result.pr_body == data.pr_body
    assert result.findings == data.findings


def test_read_missing_file_returns_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    assert read(tmp_path) is None


def test_read_malformed_json_returns_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / HANDOFF_FILE).write_text("not valid json{{{")
    assert read(tmp_path) is None


def test_read_wrong_top_level_type_returns_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / HANDOFF_FILE).write_text(json.dumps([1, 2, 3]))
    assert read(tmp_path) is None


def test_read_missing_pr_title_returns_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / HANDOFF_FILE).write_text(
        json.dumps({"pr_body": "body", "findings": {"critical": 0, "required": 0}})
    )
    assert read(tmp_path) is None


def test_read_missing_pr_body_returns_none(tmp_path: Path) -> None:
    (tmp_path / HANDOFF_FILE).write_text(
        json.dumps({"pr_title": "title", "findings": {"critical": 0, "required": 0}})
    )
    assert read(tmp_path) is None

    return {

        "identifier": "ABA-999",
        "title": "Fixture title — drain a trivial issue",
        "description": "Fixture body.\n\nMultiple paragraphs preserved verbatim.",
        "priority": 3,
        "sortOrder": 1.0,
        "state": {"type": "unstarted", "name": "Todo"},
    }


def _positions(text: str, *needles: str) -> list[int]:
    """Return the index of each needle, asserting each one is present."""
    found = []
    for needle in needles:
        idx = text.find(needle)
        assert idx != -1, f"missing segment: {needle!r}\n--- prompt ---\n{text}"
        found.append(idx)
    return found


def test_prompt_contains_four_segments_in_order(tmp_path: Path) -> None:

  "content": "\"\"\"Handoff artefact a stack-mode worker leaves behind after submitting PRs.\n\nIn stack mode the worker drives PR submission itself via the ``pr-finishing``\nskill, which runs ``gt``/``gh`` inside the worktree, posts the Linear\nreview-summary comment, and records the submitted PRs in\n``.drain-handoff.json`` as a ``pr_urls`` list. The orchestrator reads that\nlist back as its confirmation signal — a present, non-empty ``pr_urls`` means\nthe skill submitted at least one PR; its absence means submission never\ncompleted and the per-repo chain must halt rather than march on.\n\n``read`` never raises: a missing or malformed file returns ``None`` so callers\ncan treat it as \"not submitted yet.\"\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHANDOFF_FILE = \".drain-handoff.json\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass PullRequest:\n    title: str\n    url: str\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HandoffData:\n    pr_urls: tuple[PullRequest, ...]\n\n\ndef write(worktree: Path, data: HandoffData) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Serialise ``data`` to ``<worktree>/.drain-handoff.json``.\"\"\"\n    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE\n    path.write_text(\n        json.dumps(\n            {\n                \"pr_urls\": [\n                    {\"title\": pr.title, \"url\": pr.url} for pr in data.pr_urls\n                ],\n            },\n            indent=2,\n        )\n    )\n\n\ndef read(worktree: Path) -> HandoffData | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the handoff data for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent or invalid.\n\n    A valid handoff has a ``pr_urls`` list with at least one entry, each entry\n    a dict carrying string ``title`` and ``url``. An empty list is treated as\n    invalid: the skill writes ``pr_urls`` only after a PR is actually created,\n    so \"present but empty\" means no submission happened.\n    \"\"\"\n    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())\n    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        return None\n    raw = payload.get(\"pr_urls\")\n    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw:\n        return None\n    prs: list[PullRequest] = []\n    for entry in raw:\n        if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n            return None\n        title = entry.get(\"title\")\n        url = entry.get(\"url\")\n        if not isinstance(title, str) or not isinstance(url, str) or not url:\n            return None\n        prs.append(PullRequest(title=title, url=url))\n    return HandoffData(pr_urls=tuple(prs))\n"
}

  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/drain-cycle/drain_cycle/handoff.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Handoff artefact a stack-mode worker leaves behind after submitting PRs.\n\nIn stack mode the worker drives PR submission itself via the ``pr-finishing``\nskill, which runs ``gt``/``gh`` inside the worktree, posts the Linear\nreview-summary comment, and records the submitted PRs in\n``.drain-handoff.json`` as a ``pr_urls`` list. The orchestrator reads that\nlist back as its confirmation signal — a present, non-empty ``pr_urls`` means\nthe skill submitted at least one PR; its absence means submission never\ncompleted and the per-repo chain must halt rather than march on.\n\n``read`` never raises: a missing or malformed file returns ``None`` so callers\ncan treat it as \"not submitted yet.\"\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHANDOFF_FILE = \".drain-handoff.json\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass PullRequest:\n    title: str\n    url: str\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HandoffData:\n    pr_urls: tuple[PullRequest, ...]\n\n\ndef write(worktree: Path, data: HandoffData) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Serialise ``data`` to ``<worktree>/.drain-handoff.json``.\"\"\"\n    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE\n    path.write_text(\n        json.dumps(\n            {\n                \"pr_urls\": [\n                    {\"title\": pr.title, \"url\": pr.url} for pr in data.pr_urls\n                ],\n            },\n            indent=2,\n        )\n    )\n\n\ndef read(worktree: Path) -> HandoffData | None:\n    \"\"\"Return the handoff data for ``worktree``, or ``None`` if absent or invalid.\n\n    A valid handoff has a ``pr_urls`` list with at least one entry, each entry\n    a dict carrying string ``title`` and ``url``. An empty list is treated as\n    invalid: the skill writes ``pr_urls`` only after a PR is actually created,\n    so \"present but empty\" means no submission happened.\n    \"\"\"\n    path = worktree / HANDOFF_FILE\n    try:\n        payload = json.loads(path.read_text())\n    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n        return None\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        return None\n    raw = payload.get(\"pr_urls\")\n    if not isinstance(raw, list) or not raw:\n        return None\n    prs: list[PullRequest] = []\n    for entry in raw:\n        if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n            return None\n        title = entry.get(\"title\")\n        url = entry.get(\"url\")\n        if not isinstance(title, str) or not isinstance(url, str) or not url:\n            return None\n        prs.append(PullRequest(title=title, url=url))\n    return HandoffData(pr_urls=tuple(prs))\n"
}
```

38	
39	## Comments
40	
41	A code comment explains the code **as it stands** — an invariant, a non-obvious constraint, a workaround the next reader would otherwise be surprised by. It does not narrate how the code came to look this way.
42	
43	Do not write, in any comment or docstring:
44	
45	- Linear issue / ticket IDs (`ABA-NNN`), user-story labels (`US-A`…`US-D`), or task numbers (`Task 3`, `AC2`).
46	- Commit SHAs or PR numbers.
47	- Fix-history narration: "added for…", "fixes the bug where…", "regression caused by…", "previously this…", dates an issue was discovered.
48	
49	That context belongs in the commit message, the PR description, the Linear issue, or `docs/design-decisions.md` — durable artefacts that carry process history without rotting into the code. A comment pointing at a closed ticket or a squashed commit is noise to everyone who reads the code later.
50	
51	When you find an existing comment that breaks this: strip the reference and keep the explanatory prose; reword if the label was the grammatical subject; inline the explanation if it lived inside the parenthetical; delete the whole comment if nothing of value remains.
52	
53	Not covered by this rule — leave these alone: identifiers used as **sample data** in tests (`_issue("ABA-1", …)`, `worktree_path=".../ABA-A"`) and comments describing that data; schema/format placeholders (`"issue_identifier": "ABA-NNN"`); and references to in-repo docs (`README §1`, `docs/design-decisions.md`).
54	
55	## Git
56	
57	Conventional-commit-ish prefixes: `feat:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, `docs:`, `refactor:`. Subject line ≤ 70 chars; details in the body if needed. Do not add `Co-Authored-By` trailers.
58

24	
25	1. **Review** — Run `/code-review-and-quality` against the working-tree changes.
26	2. **Fix** — Address any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings are at the agent's discretion (fix or note in the summary comment).
27	3. **Commit + push** — The path here branches by how the issue is being executed:
28	   - **Direct mode** (interactive session, not spawned by drain-cycle): commit the reviewed version and push to main. This repo pushes directly to main; PRs only when the owner asks. An issue isn't Done if work only exists locally.
29	   - **Drain mode** (spawned by the drain-cycle orchestrator in a worktree): commit to the issue branch **without pushing** and write `.drain-handoff.json` (PR title, body, findings) — the worker prompt carries the exact steps. The orchestrator assembles the branch into the per-repo Graphite stack, submits the PR, and posts the PR link back to the Linear issue after the issue is confirmed Done — the agent does not run `gt`/`gh` and does not post the PR link itself.
30	4. **Summary comment** — Post a short comment on the Linear issue via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` with the review summary (count of findings by severity, plus what was fixed vs deferred). In drain mode the orchestrator appends the PR link as a separate comment; the agent's comment is the review summary only.
31	5. **Done** — Transition to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue`.
32	
33	Status updates happen at the moment of state change — not batched at end of session.
34	
35	**Blocked** = leave In Progress + add a blocker comment naming the blocker. Don't silently park work.
36	
37	**New work surfaced mid-flight** becomes a new Linear issue, slotted into a cycle deliberately. Don't silently expand scope.
```

body

---

Execution instructions:
- Working directory: /x/.worktrees/ABA-388
- Base branch: main
- Completion sequence for issue ABA-388 (run in this order, before marking Done):
  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.
  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings are at your discretion.
  3. Commit the changes to the issue branch as reviewable slices — one logical change per commit. Do not push by hand.
  4. Run `/shape:pr-finishing`. It submits the slices as stacked PR(s) via Graphite, writes the submitted PR URLs into `.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`), and posts the review-summary comment on the Linear issue. Do not run `gt`/`gh` by hand or write `.drain-handoff.json` yourself — the skill owns both.
  5. Confirm `.drain-handoff.json` now contains a non-empty `pr_urls` list. If the skill could not submit, leave the issue In Progress and comment the blocker — do not mark Done.
  6. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` (state: "Done").

before marking Done: review the changes, fix Critical/Required findings, commit them as reviewable slices on the issue branch, then run `/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) and post the review-summary comment, and only then transition to Done.

## Increment 2 — true cross-issue Graphite chaining

### What Increment 1 already gives you

Each drained issue lands **its own PR off `main`**. If a cycle has ABA-386, 387, 388 that touch the same repo, you get three independent PRs, each diffed against `main`. The worker now actually submits (Increment 1 fixed the "committed locally, no PR" bug).

### What's still missing

Those three PRs are **not stacked on each other**. If 387 builds on code that 386 introduced, 387's PR will either show 386's changes as part of its own diff (noisy, un-reviewable) or fail to apply cleanly. There's no baton passing one issue's branch as the base of the next.

### The plan — four moving parts

1. **Orchestrator base-branch baton.** Walk `plan.order` per repo and thread the previous issue's branch as the next worktree's base: `worktree.ensure(repo, id, base=prev_branch)`. The branch name is deterministic (= the issue identifier), so 387's worktree is cut from `ABA-386` instead of `main`, 388 from `ABA-387`, etc.

2. **Teach `pr-finishing` a `base` param.** Today the skill slices `git log main..HEAD`. With a baton it must slice `<base>..HEAD` so 387's PR contains *only* 387's commits, stacked on 386's PR. This change lives in the **agent-skills-shaper repo**, not here.

3. **Restore the chain-halt that #14 deleted.** If an issue produces no `pr_urls` (submission failed), the orchestrator must leave it In Progress and **stop the per-repo chain** — don't cut 387's worktree from a branch that was never pushed. Fail closed.

4. **Orchestrator reads `pr_urls` back** for the runlog / confirmation (the handoff schema already carries this after Increment 1).

### Why do it

The whole point of stack mode (per `[[drain-output-to-stacked-prs]]` and `[[stacked-pr-feature-tickets]]`) is reviewable, dependency-ordered PRs. Without chaining, multi-issue cycles in a shared repo produce PRs you can't actually review in isolation — defeating the feature you built the worktree/handoff machinery for.

### Consequence of not doing it

- Drain-cycle stays usable, but **only safely for one-issue-per-repo cycles**. The moment two issues in a cycle touch the same repo and depend on each other, the second PR's diff is wrong.
- You'd keep hand-rebasing/stacking PRs after every multi-issue drain — exactly the manual shepherding this project exists to eliminate.
- The deleted chain-halt means a *silent* failure mode survives: a failed submission could let the next issue base itself on a phantom branch.

### Scope note

Two of the four parts (orchestrator baton + chain-halt + runlog read) are in *this* repo; the `base` param is in the **shaper repo**. So Increment 2 spans two repos and wants its own Linear issues filed before building.

1	"""Thin wrapper around ``git worktree``.
2	
3	Each issue gets ``.worktrees/<issue-identifier>/`` branched off ``main``,
4	used once, then removed on Done — or preserved on halt so a later re-run
5	can resume against the committed work (see ``docs/design-decisions.md``
6	§14).
7	
8	``git worktree`` stderr is captured and surfaced in the raised
9	``RuntimeError`` on failure. The orchestrator's pre-spawn try/except
10	threads the message into the runlog's ``halt_reason`` so the operator
11	sees git's actual diagnostic (dirty tree, branch already exists,
12	missing ``main``) rather than just a non-zero exit code.
13	"""
14	from __future__ import annotations
15	
16	import os
17	import shutil
18	import subprocess
19	from dataclasses import dataclass
20	from pathlib import Path
21	from typing import Iterable
22	
23	from . import telemetry
24	
25	BASE_BRANCH = "main"
26	WORKTREE_DIR = ".worktrees"
27	
28	
29	@dataclass(frozen=True)
30	class WorktreeHandle:
31	    """A prepared worktree, together with whether it was reused.
32	
33	    ``resumed`` is ``True`` when ``ensure`` found a pre-existing worktree
34	    registered at the expected path (typically left behind by an earlier
35	    halted run). Callers thread the flag into the spawn-time prompt so
36	    the agent knows it is continuing from prior committed work rather
37	    than starting fresh.
38	    """
39	
40	    path: Path
41	    resumed: bool
42	
43	
44	def add(repo: Path, identifier: str, base: str = BASE_BRANCH) -> Path:
45	    """Create a worktree branched off ``base`` for ``identifier``.
46	
47	    Returns the absolute path to the new worktree.
48	    """
49	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
50	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.add") as span:
51	        span.set_attribute("worktree.identifier", identifier)
52	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
53	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
54	        span.set_attribute("worktree.base", base)
55	        _run_git(
56	            ["worktree", "add", "-b", identifier, str(worktree_path), base],
57	            cwd=repo,
58	        )
59	    return worktree_path
60	
61	
62	def ensure(repo: Path, identifier: str, base: str = BASE_BRANCH) -> WorktreeHandle:
63	    """Reuse a preserved worktree if one is already registered, else add.
64	
65	    A worktree registered at ``repo/.worktrees/<identifier>`` is reused
66	    as-is — no mutating git command is run, so a dirty index, staged or
67	    untracked files, and the gitignored config symlinks all survive
68	    untouched. Any other state (no entry at that path) falls through to
69	    :func:`add`, whose ``RuntimeError`` on a leftover branch or orphan
70	    directory is what the orchestrator's existing pre-spawn handler
71	    turns into the clean ``Halt: … — setup failed: …`` line.
72	    """
73	    worktree_path = repo / WORKTREE_DIR / identifier
74	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.ensure") as span:
75	        span.set_attribute("worktree.identifier", identifier)
76	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
77	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
78	        if _is_registered_worktree(repo, worktree_path):
79	            span.set_attribute("worktree.resumed", True)
80	            return WorktreeHandle(path=worktree_path, resumed=True)
81	        span.set_attribute("worktree.resumed", False)
82	    return WorktreeHandle(path=add(repo, identifier, base), resumed=False)
83	
84	
85	def link_project_config(
86	    repo: Path, worktree_path: Path, names: Iterable[str]
87	) -> list[Path]:
88	    """Symlink gitignored project-scoped config from ``repo`` into the worktree.
89	
90	    A git worktree checks out only tracked files, so gitignored project config
91	    (``.claude/`` settings/hooks/agents/skills, a root ``.mcp.json``) is absent.
92	    Linking the repo's real entries in gives a worker the same settings, hooks,
93	    agents, skills, and MCP config as an interactive session at the repo root —
94	    and because the link points at the live dir, a stateful hook reads and
95	    writes the repo's actual config exactly as a non-worktree run would.
96	
97	    For each name: skip it if absent in ``repo`` (a clean no-op for repos
98	    without that config) or if something already occupies that path in the
99	    worktree (a tracked entry git checked out, or a pre-existing link). The
100	    check uses ``os.path.lexists`` so a dangling link counts as present and is
101	    never clobbered.
102	
103	    Exception: if the worktree path is a real directory (not a symlink), it
104	    was likely created by a tool running in the worktree before the symlink
105	    could be planted (e.g. entire.io creating ``.entire/`` on first run). In
106	    that case, merge any files from the worktree directory into ``source``
107	    (without overwriting existing files), remove the directory, and replace it
108	    with a symlink so future writes land in the shared location.
109	
110	    Returns the links created.
111	    """
112	    created: list[Path] = []
113	    repo = repo.resolve()
114	    for name in names:
115	        source = repo / name
116	        if not source.exists():
117	            continue
118	        link = worktree_path / name
119	        if os.path.islink(link):
120	            continue
121	        if link.is_dir() and _is_gitignored(worktree_path, name):
122	            # Migrate: move files not already in source, then replace with symlink.
123	            for item in link.iterdir():
124	                dest = source / item.name
125	                if not dest.exists():
126	                    shutil.move(str(item), str(dest))
127	            shutil.rmtree(link)
128	        elif os.path.lexists(link):
129	            # A tracked or otherwise pre-existing entry we won't clobber.
130	            continue
131	        os.symlink(source, link)
132	        created.append(link)
133	    return created
134	
135	
136	def merge_entire_sessions(worktree_path: Path, repo: Path) -> None:
137	    """Merge session dirs from a worktree-local ``.entire/`` into the repo-root one.
138	
139	    Called just before removal so entire.io session data written by the
140	    worker isn't lost. No-op if the worktree's ``.entire`` is a symlink
141	    (already points at the repo root) or if the repo has no ``.entire/``.
142	    """
143	    worktree_entire = worktree_path / ".entire"
144	    if not worktree_entire.is_dir() or os.path.islink(worktree_entire):
145	        return
146	    repo_entire = repo.resolve() / ".entire"
147	    if not repo_entire.exists():
148	        return
149	    for item in worktree_entire.iterdir():
150	        dest = repo_entire / item.name
151	        if not dest.exists():
152	            shutil.move(str(item), str(dest))
153	
154	
155	def remove(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> None:
156	    """Remove a worktree previously created by :func:`add`."""
157	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.worktree.remove") as span:
158	        span.set_attribute("worktree.repo", repo.name)
159	        span.set_attribute("worktree.path", str(worktree_path))
160	        _run_git(["worktree", "remove", "--force", str(worktree_path)], cwd=repo)
161	
162	
163	def _is_gitignored(repo: Path, name: str) -> bool:
164	    """Return ``True`` if ``name`` is gitignored in ``repo``."""
165	    result = subprocess.run(
166	        ["git", "check-ignore", "-q", name],
167	        cwd=repo,
168	        check=False,
169	        capture_output=True,
170	    )
171	    return result.returncode == 0
172	
173	
174	def _is_registered_worktree(repo: Path, worktree_path: Path) -> bool:
175	    """Return ``True`` if git lists a worktree at ``worktree_path``.
176	
177	    Parses ``git worktree list --porcelain -z`` for a ``worktree <path>``
178	    record matching ``worktree_path.resolve()``. ``-z`` makes each record
179	    NUL-separated and each field NUL-terminated, so paths with embedded
180	    spaces or newlines are unambiguous. The resolve step matches git's
181	    own canonicalisation (symlinks, ``..``) so a worktree under a
182	    symlinked repo path still matches the entry git printed. A non-zero
183	    git exit is treated as not-registered so ``ensure`` falls through to
184	    ``add``, whose error surfaces git's real diagnostic via the
185	    orchestrator's pre-spawn halt path.
186	    """
187	    target = worktree_path.resolve()
188	    result = subprocess.run(
189	        ["git", "worktree", "list", "--porcelain", "-z"],
190	        cwd=repo,
191	        check=False,
192	        capture_output=True,
193	        text=True,
194	    )
195	    if result.returncode != 0:
196	        return False
197	    for field in result.stdout.split("\0"):
198	        if not field.startswith("worktree "):
199	            continue
200	        listed = Path(field.removeprefix("worktree "))
201	        if listed.resolve() == target:
202	            return True
203	    return False
204	
205	
206	def _run_git(args: list[str], *, cwd: Path) -> None:
207	    result = subprocess.run(
208	        ["git", *args],
209	        cwd=cwd,
210	        check=False,
211	        capture_output=True,
212	        text=True,
213	    )
214	    if result.returncode != 0:
215	        raise RuntimeError(
216	            f"git {' '.join(args)} failed (exit {result.returncode}): "
217	            f"{result.stderr.strip() or result.stdout.strip() or '<no output>'}"
218	        )
219	
```

393	            return halt_code  # type: ignore[return-value]
394	
395	        # Cycle-wide circuit breaker: every issue may stay under its own
396	        # per-issue caps while their sum drains the quota. Check the

398	        # before spawning the next one; on breach, stop the cycle.

411	            return 1

414	    cycle_span.set_attribute("drain.outcome", "drained")

416	    return 0

435	    Returns ``(halt_code, pane_id)`` where ``halt_code`` is ``None`` when the
436	    issue reached Done (the caller then runs the cycle-wide circuit breaker and
437	    proceeds to the next issue), or an exit code when the run must halt.
438	    ``pane_id`` is the tmux pane ID opened for this issue (or ``None``).
439	    Each halt path is also marked on the span with a static ``exception.slug``.

796	        tripped = stop_guard.read_tripped(worktree_path)
797	        if tripped is not None:
798	            halt_reason += f" — {stop_guard.TRIPPED_HALT_REASON}: {tripped}"
799	        if revert_error is not None:
800	            halt_reason += (
801	                f" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}"
802	            )
803	        # halt_reason carries the same string also printed to stderr below
804	        # so the on-disk and terminal surfaces cannot drift.
805	        log.append_entry(
806	            issue_identifier=identifier,
807	            started_at=started_at,
808	            finished_at=finished_at,
809	            exit_code=result.exit_code,
810	            final_linear_state=effective_state,
811	            worktree_path=str(worktree_path),
812	            halt_reason=halt_reason,
813	            flow=flow_name,
814	            outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,
815	            prep_verdict=prep_verdict,
816	            responder_runs=responder_runs,
817	            shape_task_invoked=is_verify,
818	            **_worker_log_fields(result),
819	        )
820	        issue_span.set_attribute("issue.final_linear_state", effective_state)
821	        if outcome_verdict is not None:
822	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.outcome_verdict", outcome_verdict["result"])
823	        if prep_verdict is not None:
824	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.prep_verdict", prep_verdict["result"])
825	        if flow_name is not None:
826	            issue_span.set_attribute("issue.responder_run_count", len(responder_runs))
827	        telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, "err-issue-not-done", halt_reason)
828	        console.halt(halt_reason)
829	        return 1, pane_id
830

1	"""Prompt builder for spawned ``claude -p`` sessions.
2	
3	The prompt is the entire contract between the orchestrator and the spawned
4	agent — there's no system prompt, no multi-turn loop. The four-segment
5	ordering below is load-bearing: the agent reads top-down, so context
6	(title + body) comes before instructions (preamble + tail), and the tail
7	line is last so it stays in the trailing-tokens window the model attends
8	to most strongly.
9	"""
10	from __future__ import annotations
11	
12	from pathlib import Path
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	_VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL = "verify"
16	
17	
18	def is_verify_flow(issue: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
19	    """Return True when the issue carries the ``verify`` label."""
20	    return _VERIFY_FLOW_LABEL in issue.get("labels", [])
21	
22	
23	def _shape_task_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
24	    """Shape-task preamble for verify-flow worker sessions.
25	
26	    Instructs the worker to check for an existing Task Shaper output
27	    block in the Linear issue body before invoking ``/shape:task``. The
28	    check-first approach handles both fresh sessions (block absent →
29	    invoke) and §14 resumes (block present → reuse).
30	    """
31	    return (
32	        f"Shaping step (run before implementing): fetch the Linear issue body "
33	        f"for {identifier} and check for a Task Shaper output block "
34	        "(delimited by `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-START -->` and `<!-- TASK-SHAPER-END -->`). "
35	        "If the block is present, use it as your implementation guide and skip "
36	        "re-running `/shape:task`. If the block is absent, run "
37	        f"`/shape:task {identifier}` first and use its output as your "
38	        "implementation guide.\n\n"
39	    )
40	
41	
42	_TAIL = (
43	    "before marking Done: review the working-tree changes for correctness "
44	    "and quality, fix Critical/Required findings, commit + push, then post a "
45	    "review-summary comment on the issue and transition to Done."
46	)
47	
48	_STACK_TAIL = (
49	    "before marking Done: review the changes, fix Critical/Required findings, "
50	    "commit them as reviewable slices on the issue branch, then run "
51	    "`/shape:pr-finishing` to submit the stacked PR(s) and post the "
52	    "review-summary comment, and only then transition to Done."
53	)
54	
55	
56	def _resume_directive(identifier: str) -> str:
57	    """Resume preamble for a worktree carrying prior committed work.
58	
59	    Inserted as the first line inside the preamble (after the ``---``
60	    separator, before "Execution instructions:") so the agent reads it
61	    ahead of the procedure but the tail still holds the last-line
62	    position the four-segment ordering reserves for it.
63	    """
64	    return (
65	        f"Resuming issue {identifier}: this worktree carries prior committed "
66	        "work from an earlier session that was halted. Run "
67	        "`git log --oneline main..HEAD` and `git status` first to read what "
68	        "is already done, then continue from that point — do not restart "
69	        "from scratch.\n\n"
70	    )
71	
72	
73	def _normal_preamble(
74	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
75	) -> str:
76	    return (
77	        "---\n\n"
78	        f"{resume_segment}"
79	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
80	        "Execution instructions:\n"
81	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
82	        "- Base branch: main\n"
83	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
84	        "before marking Done):\n"
85	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
86	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
87	        "are at your discretion.\n"
88	        "  3. Commit and push to main.\n"
89	        "  4. Post a short review-summary comment on the Linear issue via "
90	        "`mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_comment` (count of findings by severity, "
91	        "fixed vs deferred).\n"
92	        "  5. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
93	        '(state: "Done").\n'
94	    )
95	
96	
97	def _stack_preamble(
98	    identifier: str, worktree: Path, resume_segment: str, shape_task_segment: str
99	) -> str:
100	    return (
101	        "---\n\n"
102	        f"{resume_segment}"
103	        f"{shape_task_segment}"
104	        "Execution instructions:\n"
105	        f"- Working directory: {worktree}\n"
106	        "- Base branch: main\n"
107	        f"- Completion sequence for issue {identifier} (run in this order, "
108	        "before marking Done):\n"
109	        "  1. Review the working-tree changes for correctness and quality.\n"
110	        "  2. Fix any Critical or Required findings. Lower-severity findings "
111	        "are at your discretion.\n"
112	        "  3. Commit the changes to the issue branch as reviewable slices — "
113	        "one logical change per commit. Do not push by hand.\n"
114	        "  4. Run `/shape:pr-finishing`. It submits the slices as stacked "
115	        "PR(s) via Graphite, writes the submitted PR URLs into "
116	        "`.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`), and posts the review-summary "
117	        "comment on the Linear issue. Do not run `gt`/`gh` by hand or write "
118	        "`.drain-handoff.json` yourself — the skill owns both.\n"
119	        "  5. Confirm `.drain-handoff.json` now contains a non-empty `pr_urls` "
120	        "list. If the skill could not submit, leave the issue In Progress and "
121	        "comment the blocker — do not mark Done.\n"
122	        "  6. Transition issue to Done via `mcp__claude_ai_Linear__save_issue` "
123	        '(state: "Done").\n'
124	    )
125	
126	
127	def build(
128	    issue: dict[str, Any],
129	    worktree: Path,
130	    *,
131	    resumed: bool = False,
132	    stack: bool = False,
133	) -> str:
134	    title = issue.get("title", "")
135	    description = issue.get("description") or ""
136	    identifier = issue.get("identifier", "")
137	
138	    resume_segment = _resume_directive(identifier) if resumed else ""
139	    shape_task_segment = _shape_task_directive(identifier) if is_verify_flow(issue) else ""
140	
141	    if stack:
142	        preamble = _stack_preamble(identifier, worktree, resume_segment, shape_task_segment)
143	        tail = _STACK_TAIL
144	    else:
145	        preamble = _normal_preamble(identifier, worktree, resume_segment, shape_task_segment)
146	        tail = _TAIL
147	
148	    return (
149	        f"# {title}\n\n"
150	        f"{description}\n\n"
151	        f"{preamble}\n"
152	        f"{tail}\n"
153	    )
154	
```

> AGENT

Quick answer to your question, then I'll continue the build.

**Why `incremental-implementation` and not `shape:build`:** Honestly, two reasons — one principled, one a gap.

1. **Principled:** the task you gave me is *execution*, not *shaping*. The shape is already settled — it's spec'd in `stack-pr-submission-regression.md` (the four parts), grounded in the code I just read. What's left is to write it in safe, tested slices across two files. `incremental-implementation` is exactly that discipline: implement → test → verify → commit per slice. `shape:*` skills are for turning an under-specified idea into a buildable spec — which we already did last session via `idea-refine` + your AskUserQuestion decisions.

2. **The gap:** when I searched for a skill, I grepped the agent-skills plugin cache for `shape|task|plan|spec|implement` and matched the planning/implementation family there. I did *not* enumerate the full `shape:*` skill set from the shaper plugin — I only knew `shape:task` and `shape:pr-finishing` because the codebase references them. So `shape:build` wasn't in front of me to weigh. If it's the house convention for "now build the spec," that's a fair call you might prefer — but for this specific task (no shaping left, pure tested execution) I'd still lean `incremental-implementation`.

If you'd rather I drive the rest through `shape:build`, say so and I'll switch. Otherwise I'll keep going — mid-slice-1 right now.

{
  "replace_all": false,
