  "new_string": "        if is_done:\n            # Stack-mode confirmation gate, read before teardown removes the\n            # worktree. A Done issue in stack mode must have left a non-empty\n            # ``pr_urls`` — that is the orchestrator's proof submission ran.\n            # If it's missing, the worker marked Done without opening a PR\n            # (the regression this feature closes): revert + halt, preserve\n            # the worktree for inspection, and do NOT extend the baton — the\n            # next same-repo issue must not stack onto a branch that was\n            # never pushed.\n            submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path) if stack else None\n            if stack and submitted is None:\n                original_state_name = issue[\"state\"][\"name\"]\n                effective_state, revert_error = _revert_to_pre_halt_state(\n                    issue[\"id\"],\n                    target_state_name=original_state_name,\n                    pre_revert_state_name=post_spawn_state,\n                )\n                halt_reason = (\n                    f\"{_halt_message(identifier, effective_state, worktree_path)}\"\n                    \" — marked Done but .drain-handoff.json has no submitted \"\n                    \"pr_urls; stack chain halted\"\n                )\n                if revert_error is not None:\n                    halt_reason += (\n                        f\" — revert to {original_state_name!r} failed: {revert_error}\"\n                    )\n                log.append_entry(\n                    issue_identifier=identifier,\n                    started_at=started_at,\n                    finished_at=finished_at,\n                    exit_code=result.exit_code,\n                    final_linear_state=effective_state,\n                    worktree_path=str(worktree_path),\n                    halt_reason=halt_reason,\n                    flow=flow_name,\n                    outcome_verdict=outcome_verdict,\n                    prep_verdict=prep_verdict,\n                    responder_runs=responder_runs,\n                    shape_task_invoked=is_verify,\n                    **_worker_log_fields(result),\n                )\n                issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.final_linear_state\", effective_state)\n                telemetry.mark_error(issue_span, \"err-stack-no-prs\", halt_reason)\n                console.halt(halt_reason)\n                return 1, pane_id\n\n            if submitted is not None:\n                # Hand the baton to the next same-repo issue and record the\n                # submitted PRs as the orchestrator's confirmation line.\n                repo_baton[target_repo.name] = identifier\n                issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.submitted_pr_count\", len(submitted.pr_urls))\n                console.worker_event(\n                    identifier,\n                    \"submitted \"\n                    + \", \".join(pr.url for pr in submitted.pr_urls),\n                )\n\n            issue_span.set_attribute(\"issue.final_linear_state\", post_spawn_state)\n\n            remove_error: str | None = None\n            try:\n                worktree.merge_entire_sessions(worktree_path, target_repo)\n                worktree.remove(target_repo, worktree_path)"

491	def _write_fake_claude_script(tmp_path: Path, done_marker: Path) -> Path:
492	    """A no-op stand-in for ``claude -p``.

300	
301	def run(
302	    repos: Repos,
303	    limits: Limits | None = None,
304	    *,
305	    watch: bool = False,
306	    no_stack: bool = False,
307	) -> int:
308	    """Drain the current cycle inside the ``drain.cycle`` root span.
309	
310	    The span wrapper is thin so the body keeps its shape; per-issue work nests
311	    under it via ``_drain_one_issue``'s ``drain.issue`` spans, and the Linear,
312	    worktree, and worker spans nest under those — yielding one trace per drain.
313	    """
314	    if limits is None:
315	        limits = Limits()
316	    with telemetry.tracer.start_as_current_span("drain.cycle") as cycle_span:
317	        return _run(repos, limits, cycle_span, watch=watch, no_stack=no_stack)

1	"""Multi-repo orchestrator behaviour.
2	
3	Two halves:
4	
5	1. **Happy path** — two issues with different ``repo:`` labels are each
6	   resolved to their respective configured paths, and ``subprocess.run``
7	   sees the matching ``cwd``. Guards against a regression that wires
8	   ``Path.cwd()`` back in (or hardcodes one of the issues' repos for
9	   both).
10	
11	2. **Resolution halt** — each ``RepoResolutionError`` variant must
12	   write a run-log entry and emit the matching ``Halt:`` line, and
13	   must **not** call ``linear.set_state`` (no state was moved yet).
14	   Behaviour contrast with the existing setup-failure halt: that one
15	   also doesn't revert, but it does try ``set_state`` first; here we
16	   never reach it.

  "new_string": "    main_b = _git_log_hashes(repo_b, \"main\")\n    hashes_3 = _git_log_hashes(repo_b, \"ABA-3\")\n    assert main_b[0] in hashes_3\n\n\ndef test_stack_mode_done_without_handoff_halts_and_preserves_worktree(\n    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A stack-mode issue the worker marked Done but left no submitted\n    ``pr_urls`` must halt the run rather than tear down: the orchestrator\n    cannot let the next same-repo issue stack onto a branch that was never\n    pushed. The worktree is preserved for inspection and the run exits\n    non-zero.\"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    repo.mkdir()\n    _init_repo(repo)\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", str(tmp_path))\n\n    issue = _issue(\"ABA-1\", repo_name=\"alpha\", sort_order=1.0)\n    issues_by_id = {issue[\"id\"]: issue}\n    marker = tmp_path / \"done.txt\"\n\n    def fake_pending_issues(cycle_id: str):\n        completed = set(_lines(marker))\n        return linear._plan(\n            [i for i in [issue] if i[\"identifier\"] not in completed]\n        )\n\n    def fake_get_issue(issue_id: str) -> dict:\n        if issues_by_id[issue_id][\"identifier\"] in set(_lines(marker)):\n            return {**issues_by_id[issue_id], \"state\": {\"type\": \"completed\", \"name\": \"Done\"}}\n        return issues_by_id[issue_id]\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, \"current_cycle_id\", lambda: \"cycle-id\")\n    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, \"pending_issues\", fake_pending_issues)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, \"get_issue\", fake_get_issue)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(linear, \"set_state\", lambda issue_id, name: None)\n\n    # Worker marks the issue Done but writes no .drain-handoff.json.\n    script = tmp_path / \"no-handoff-claude.sh\"\n    script.write_text(\n        \"#!/bin/sh\\n\"\n        f'printf \"%s\\\\n\" \"$(basename \"$PWD\")\" >> \"{marker}\"\\n'\n    )\n    script.chmod(0o755)\n    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(script)])\n\n    exit_code = orchestrator.run(repos.Repos(mapping={\"alpha\": repo}))\n\n    assert exit_code != 0\n    # Worktree is preserved (not torn down) so the work can be inspected.\n    assert (repo / \".worktrees\" / \"ABA-1\").exists()\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Resolution-halt path.   "new_string": "   - **Drain mode** (spawned by the drain-cycle orchestrator in a worktree): commit the reviewed changes to the issue branch as reviewable slices (one logical change per commit), then run `/shape:pr-finishing`. That skill owns submission — it drives `gt`/`gh` to submit the stacked PR(s), writes the submitted PR URLs into `.drain-handoff.json` (`pr_urls`), and posts the review-summary comment on the Linear issue. The worker does not run `gt`/`gh` by hand, does not push by hand, and does not hand-author `.drain-handoff.json`. The orchestrator no longer assembles or submits anything; it reads `pr_urls` back as its confirmation that submission succeeded — and a Done stack-mode issue with no `pr_urls` halts the run rather than letting the next issue stack on an unpushed branch. When the prompt names a base branch other than `main` (the orchestrator chains consecutive same-repo issues so each worktree branches off the prior issue's branch), pass that base to `/shape:pr-finishing` so it slices `<base>..HEAD` rather than `main..HEAD`."
}

1\n```\n\nThe model then uses that rate directly as the base-case Y2–Y5 CAGR, with bear/bull scenarios applied as fixed multipliers.\n\nThis behaves reasonably for stable businesses, but breaks when the oldest comparison year is abn… (truncated, use `get_issue` for full description)","priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-104/spike-detect-and-handle-base-year-effect-in-stockmodel-fcf-growth-rate","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-104-spike-detect-handle-base-year-effect-in-stockmodel-fcf","createdAt":"2026-05-13T15:07:17.704Z","updatedAt":"2026-05-25T08:53:26.261Z","archivedAt":"2026-05-25T08:27:55.267Z","completedAt":"2026-05-18T09:20:45.699Z","startedAt":"2026-05-18T08:58:42.865Z","canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Done","statusType":"completed","labels":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","assignee":"Anton Babushkin","assigneeId":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Equity - {
  "title": "Teach /shape:pr-finishing a base param so it slices &lt;base&gt;..HEAD",
  "team": "Personal",
  "project": "Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills",
  "cycle": "793fc9ea-08c7-40c1-8c1b-417efb20adbf",
  "priority": 2,
  "labels": [
    "agent-skills-shaper"
  ],
  "description": "## What\n\n`/shape:pr-finishing` (in `agent-skills-shaper`) currently derives its slices from `git log main..HEAD`. Add an optional base parameter so it slices `<base>..HEAD` and opens the first PR against `<base>` instead of `main`.\n\n## Why\n\ndrain-cycle's orchestrator now chains consecutive same-repo issues: each issue's worktree branches off the prior submitted issue's branch (a true Graphite stack), not `main`. The worker prompt already tells the skill the base branch when it isn't `main`. Until the skill honours that base, a chained issue's PR slices `main..HEAD` and therefore re-includes every commit from the issues beneath it in the stack — the PR diff is wrong and un-reviewable.\n\nThis is the cross-repo half of drain-cycle Increment 2 (cross-issue Graphite chaining). The drain-cycle side (per-repo base baton, chain-halt on missing `pr_urls`, base threaded into the prompt) is already shipped to `drain-cycle` main.\n\n## Acceptance\n\n- The skill accepts a base branch (default `main`, preserving current behaviour byte-for-byte when unset).\n- With a non-`main` base it slices `<base>..HEAD` and the bottom PR of the stack targets `<base>`.\n- The worker invocation contract matches what the drain-cycle stack prompt passes (see `_stack_preamble` in `drain_cycle/prompt.py`: the prompt instructs the worker to \"pass `<base>` to the skill as its base branch\").\n- A chained two-issue stack produces two PRs whose diffs don't overlap."
}

> AGENT

Increment 2 (drain-cycle side) is built — 3 commits, 320 tests green, not yet pushed.

## What I built

**Slice 1 — `prompt.py`** (`6d242fb`): `build()` gained `base="main"`. Threaded into both preambles, the resume `git log` range, and a stack-preamble clause that tells the worker to pass a non-`main` base to `/shape:pr-finishing`. Default is byte-identical to before.

**Slice 2+3 — `orchestrator.py`** (`04d46c5`): a per-repo **base baton** (`repo_baton: dict[repo → last submitted branch]`). In stack mode each same-repo issue branches its worktree off the prior submitted issue's branch; cross-repo issues stay on `main`. Before teardown (which removes the worktree but leaves the branch ref), it reads `pr_urls` from the handoff:
- present → advance the baton, log the submitted URLs;
- **absent on a Done stack issue → revert + halt, preserve the worktree** (the restored chain-halt — won't stack the next issue on an unpushed branch).

**Tests**: rewrote the stack test to assert chaining (ABA-2 stacks on ABA-1; cross-repo ABA-3 forks main), added a chain-halt test, and put orthogonal orchestrator tests in push mode (`no_stack=True`) since they don't exercise stacking.

**Docs/tracking**: updated `AGENTS.md`; filed **[ABA-391](https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-391)** (High, agent-skills-shaper, current cycle) for the cross-repo half — the `base` param in `/shape:pr-finishing`. **Until ABA-391 ships, chaining over-slices** (`main..HEAD` re-includes lower commits), so the worktree-chaining is correct but PR diffs aren't yet — that's the gating dependency.

## Your call on two things

1. **Code review + push.** I haven't run `/code-review-and-quality` or pushed to main yet. Want me to review the diff and push the 3 commits?
2. **The `shape:build` question** — answered inline earlier: I chose `incremental-implementation` because the work was already spec'd (no shaping left), but if `shape:build` is your house convention I'm happy to use it going forward.

Confirmed. That comment line is the one AGENTS.md violation.

## Review Summary

**Verdict:** REQUEST CHANGES

**Overview:** Increment 2 cleanly adds per-repo base-branch chaining and a chain-halt gate. The core invariants (1-4) all hold: the baton advances only when `pr_urls` is present, the branch survives teardown, None-normalization is correct and mutates `baton`, and `base="main"` is byte-identical (test-proven). One real correctness gap exists on the cross-run resume path, plus one AGENTS.md comment violation. 320/320 tests pass.

### Critical Issues
None.

### Required Issues

- **drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:762** — AGENTS.md comment-rule violation. The phrase "(the regression this feature closes)" is fix-history narration, explicitly forbidden ("fixes the bug where…"). The rest of the comment is excellent and explains the invariant as-it-stands. Fix: drop the parenthetical, e.g. `# If it's missing, the worker marked Done without opening a PR: revert + halt,`.

- **drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:537 / 541 (baton reconstruction on cross-run resume)** — The `repo_baton` is rebuilt empty at the top of every `_run` (line ~373). Consider: ABA-1 submits PRs (baton[alpha]=ABA-1, worktree torn down), ABA-2 chains its worktree off branch `ABA-1`, then ABA-2 halts for an unrelated reason after its worktree is created (preserved). On the next orchestrator invocation the baton starts empty; ABA-1 is now Done and filtered out of `pending_issues`, so ABA-2's base is computed as `main` (line 541, `baton.get(name, BASE_BRANCH)`). `worktree.ensure` correctly reuses the preserved worktree and ignores `base` for the *checkout* (invariant 2/resume) — but `base` is still threaded into `prompt.build(base=base)` at line ~588. The resumed ABA-2 worker is therefore told `git log --oneline main..HEAD`, "Base branch: main", and to pass `main` to `/shape:pr-finishing`. Since ABA-2's branch is actually stacked on `ABA-1`, slicing `main..HEAD` folds ABA-1's already-submitted commits into ABA-2's PR — the exact mis-slice the chaining is designed to prevent. The happy path and single-run halt path are fine; only the multi-invocation resume-of-a-chained-issue case is wrong. Recommended fix: when `ensure` returns `resumed=True`, derive `base` from the existing branch's actual fork point (e.g. record the base in the handoff/worktree metadata at creation, or read it back) rather than from the in-memory baton, and pass that to `prompt.build`. At minimum, document this limitation in `docs/design-decisions.md` and add a resume-of-chained-issue test, since no current test exercises a second `_run` with a pre-existing chained worktree.

### Advisory

- **drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:803-812** — The baton is advanced (`baton[target_repo.name] = identifier`) before the worktree teardown (`worktree.remove`, line 819). If teardown fails, `remove_error` is recorded but the run continues and the baton already points at this issue's branch — correct, since the branch still exists. No change needed; calling it out as intentional-and-fine for the reviewer of record.

- **drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:766** — `submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path) if stack else None`. In push mode `submitted` is always None and the whole gate is skipped — correct. Worth a one-line assertion-by-comment that push-mode Done issues intentionally bypass the pr_urls gate, since the `if stack and submitted is None` / `if submitted is not None` pair reads as if it could apply outside stack mode.

### Nit

- **drain_cycle/orchestrator.py:541** — Line slightly long and the `if stack else BASE_BRANCH` ternary wrapping a `.get(...)` default is a touch dense. Optional: lift to a small helper or split for readability. Behaviour is correct.

### What's Done Well

- The chain-halt invariant is implemented exactly right: the baton is advanced **only** inside `if submitted is not None`, and the no-`pr_urls` branch returns before reaching it — there is no path that halts and advances. The accompanying `test_stack_mode_done_without_handoff_halts_and_preserves_worktree` asserts both the non-zero exit and worktree preservation.
- The `base="main"` default is genuinely byte-identical, and `test_base_defaults_to_main_byte_identical` proves it across stack/push/resume — the right way to de-risk threading a new param through three preamble builders.
- The `base_clause` is correctly suppressed for `base == "main"`, keeping the unchained stack prompt unchanged, and the resume git-log range is anchored at `base` so a chained worktree reads back only its own commits.

### Verification Story

- Tests reviewed: yes. New prompt tests cover default-identity, chained stack rendering, and push-mode base-line-only. New orchestrator tests cover same-repo chaining (ancestor assertion), cross-repo isolation (branches off its own main), and the chain-halt. Gap: no test exercises a second `_run` over a pre-existing chained worktree (the Required resume-baton issue).
- Build verified: yes — `uv run pytest -q`, 320 passed.
- Security checked: yes — no untrusted input, no secrets, no injection surface; baton keys are repo names and values are issue identifiers used as git branch names (created by the orchestrator itself).

Push-mode issues have no stack to\n            # extend and no handoff, so they bypass the gate entirely.\n            submitted = handoff.read(worktree_path) if stack else None"

154	def test_resume_reuses_preserved_worktree_and_signals_resumed_prompt(
155	    tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch
156	) -> None:
157	    """Pre-existing worktree on disk is reused; the spawned prompt carries
158	    ``resumed=True``.
159	
160	    This is the headline resume contract: a re-run after a halt does not
161	    fail on ``git worktree add`` colliding with the preserved worktree,
162	    does not require manual cleanup, and the spawned agent learns it is
163	    continuing rather than starting fresh — both halves matter, so they
164	    are pinned in one test.
165	    """
166	    repo = tmp_path / "repo"
167	    repo.mkdir()
168	    _init_repo(repo)
169	    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)
170	    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path))
171	
172	    issue = _issue("ABA-STUCK", sort_order=1.0)
173	
174	    # Pre-create the preserved worktree as if a prior run halted here.
175	    preserved = repo / ".worktrees" / issue["identifier"]
176	    subprocess.run(
177	        [
178	            "git",
179	            "worktree",
180	            "add",
181	            "-b",
182	            issue["identifier"],
183	            str(preserved),
184	            "main",
185	        ],
186	        cwd=repo,
187	        check=True,
188	        capture_output=True,
189	    )
190	    sentinel = preserved / "PRESERVED_FILE"
191	    sentinel.write_text("from the prior halt\n")
192	
193	    # Pre-seed one halted entry so the orchestrator's resume-cap sees this
194	    # as a re-run, not a first attempt.
195	    runs_dir = tmp_path / ".drain-cycle" / "runs"
196	    _seed_runlog_entry(
197	        runs_dir,
198	        cycle_id=_CYCLE_ID,
199	        identifier=issue["identifier"],
200	        final_state="Todo",
201	        suffix="prior",
202	    )
203	
204	    build_calls: list[dict] = []
205	    real_build = prompt.build
206	
207	    def recording_build(issue_arg, worktree_arg, *, resumed=False, stack=False, base="main"):
208	        build_calls.append(
209	            {"identifier": issue_arg["identifier"], "resumed": resumed}
210	        )
211	        return real_build(
212	            issue_arg, worktree_arg, resumed=resumed, stack=stack, base=base
213	        )
214	
215	    monkeypatch.setattr(prompt, "build", recording_build)
216	
217	    set_state_calls: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
218	    _patch_linear(monkeypatch, [issue], set_state_calls=set_state_calls)

220	    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, "_CLAUDE_CMD", [str(_noop_claude(tmp_path))])
221	
222	    exit_code = orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo), no_stack=True)
223	    # The no-op claude leaves Linear at Todo, so the orchestrator halts on
224	    # the not-Done branch — but the load-bearing assertions below are about
225	    # what happened BEFORE that halt: ensure reused, prompt was resumed.
226	    assert exit_code == 1
227	
228	    # The orchestrator called prompt.build with resumed=True — the resume
229	    # directive will be in the spawned-agent prompt.
230	    assert build_calls == [{"identifier": issue["identifier"], "resumed": True}]
231	
232	    # The preserved worktree's sentinel file is still there — ensure did
233	    # not blow it away or recreate the worktree (which would have wiped
234	    # the gitignored sentinel).
235	    assert sentinel.read_text() == "from the prior halt\n"
236	
237	    # Pre-spawn set_state(In Progress) still fires on a resume — the
238	    # lifecycle half-owned by the orchestrator does not change because the
239	    # worktree was reused.

The base recorded in the worktree at\n    creation must win, so the resumed worker slices ``<base>..HEAD`` rather\n    than folding the lower stack into its diff.\n    \"\"\"\n    repo = tmp_path / \"repo\"\n    repo.mkdir()\n    _init_repo(repo)\n    monkeypatch.chdir(repo)\n    monkeypatch.setenv(\"HOME\", str(tmp_path))\n\n    # A prior same-repo issue's branch the worktree was stacked on.\n    subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"branch\", \"ABA-1\", \"main\"], cwd=repo, check=True, capture_output=True\n    )\n\n    issue = _issue(\"ABA-2\", sort_order=1.0)\n    preserved = repo / \".worktrees\" / issue[\"identifier\"]\n    subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"worktree\", \"add\", \"-b\", issue[\"identifier\"], str(preserved), \"ABA-1\"],\n        cwd=repo,\n        check=True,\n        capture_output=True,\n    )\n    # The base marker ``worktree.add`` would have written at creation.\n    (preserved / worktree.BASE_FILE).write_text(\"ABA-1\\n\")\n\n    build_calls: list[dict] = []\n    real_build = prompt.build\n\n    def recording_build(issue_arg, worktree_arg, *, resumed=False, stack=False, base=\"main\"):\n        build_calls.append({\"identifier\": issue_arg[\"identifier\"], \"base\": base})\n        return real_build(\n            issue_arg, worktree_arg, resumed=resumed, stack=stack, base=base\n        )\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(prompt, \"build\", recording_build)\n    _patch_linear(monkeypatch, [issue])\n    monkeypatch.setattr(orchestrator, \"_CLAUDE_CMD\", [str(_noop_claude(tmp_path))])\n\n    # Stack mode (no no_stack): the baton starts empty, so only the recovered\n    # worktree base can supply ``ABA-1``.\n    orchestrator.run(_stub_repos(repo))\n\n    assert build_calls == [{\"identifier\": \"ABA-2\", \"base\": \"ABA-1\"}]\n\n\ndef test_resume_cap_halts_before_any_state_change("
}

```
Done. Three conservative readability simplifications, all behavior-preserving, full suite green at 323 passing.

## What I simplified

1. **orchestrator.py ~541 — `base` computation.** Replaced a 90-char inline `X if stack else Y` one-liner (which buried the `baton.get(...)` lookup behind a trailing conditional) with a plain 4-line if/else. The split makes the stack-vs-push branch read at a glance instead of requiring a left-to-right parse.

2. **orchestrator.py ~813 — submitted-PR console event.** Replaced a multi-line `"submitted " + ", ".join(...)` string concatenation passed inline to `console.worker_event` with a named `pr_list` intermediate and an f-string. Shorter and the join no longer hides as a continuation argument.

3. **prompt.py ~109 — `base_clause`.** Converted an inverted-condition ternary (empty-string-if-main / else-the-clause, the kind that needs a mental pause because the "nothing" case comes first) into a straightforward `if base == "main": ... else: ...`. Same two outcomes, read top-to-bottom.

## What I deliberately left alone

- **`repo_baton: dict | None = None` + `baton = repo_baton if repo_baton is not None else {}` (orchestrator).** This is a real defensive default for direct test callers of `_drain_one_issue`, not incidental complexity. Removing it would change the signature contract.
- **The chain-halt gate block (orchestrator ~762-817).** Long but linear — a guard-style `if stack and submitted is None:` early-return followed by the success path. Lifting any of it into a helper would add an abstraction the project's CLAUDE.md explicitly discourages, and the `log.append_entry(...)` call shares no clean seam with the rest. The explanatory comments are "why", not "what", so they stay.
- **worktree.py `read_base` / `BASE_FILE` and stop_guard.py's frozenset addition.** Already minimal and idiomatic (try/except OSError fallback, one-token set member). Nothing to cut.

## Comment-rule check

No comments reference ticket IDs, SHAs, PR numbers, or fix-history narration — none in the diff before, none introduced. Nothing to flag.

## Tests

323 passed after each edit (ran the suite three times, once per change). No test modifications.

Changes left unstaged for your review; not committed.

## Code review → fixes
The sub-agent flagged two Required issues, both fixed (`b445f12`):
1. **AGENTS.md comment violation** — removed the "(the regression this feature closes)" fix-history parenthetical.
2. **Real correctness gap** — a *resumed* chained issue recomputed its base from the in-memory baton (empty across runs), mis-slicing as `main..HEAD`. Fixed by persisting the fork branch in a `.drain-base` marker at worktree creation and reading it back on reuse. Wired into `.gitignore` + the stop-guard own-artefacts set, with a unit test and a resume-of-chained-issue orchestrator test.

The advisory (push-mode bypass clarity) got a one-line comment; the nit was handled by /simplify.

## /simplify → fixes
Three conservative, behavior-preserving readability edits (`6cba905`): unfolded the dense `base` ternary into if/else, extracted a `pr_list` intermediate, and de-inverted the `base_clause` conditional. The sub-agent correctly left the `dict | None` defensive default and the chain-halt block alone (lifting them would be premature abstraction).

644  SKILL.md  5.8K
===SKILL===
---
name: pr-finishing
description: Finish an already-green, sliced development branch by submitting one small PR per commit slice. Use when work is complete, committed in reviewable slices, and ready for PR submission through Graphite or plain git.
---

## Purpose

`pr-finishing` turns an already-green feature branch into a stack of small PRs, one per commit slice. Prefer Graphite when available and configured; otherwise use plain git. Preserve the same PR body format on both paths.

## Use when

- Work is complete and tests pass.
- Changes are committed as independently reviewable slices, one logical change per commit.
- The operator asks to finish, submit, create stacked PRs, or submit the stack.
- Optional: a drain-cycle handoff file exists at `.drain-handoff.json`.
- Optional: a Linear issue ID is available for the final trail comment.

Do not use this skill to hide failing tests, submit a known-broken branch, or re-slice a squashed multi-change commit after the fact.

## Inputs

- Feature branch with one logical change per commit.
- Optional `.drain-handoff.json` containing an `issue` and `slices`.
- Optional Linear issue ID from the branch name, handoff file, user request, or available Linear plugin.
- Optional Graphite CLI, `gt`.
- GitHub CLI, `gh`, for PR creation and verification.

Example `.drain-handoff.json`:

```json
{
  "issue": "ABC-123",
  "slices": [
    {
      "sha": "<40-char SHA>",
      "title": "<PR title>",
      "why": "<optional motivation>"
    }
  ]
}
```

## Outputs

- One PR per slice.
- Each PR body uses the same What / Why / Focus template.
- If .drain-handoff.json exists, update it with pr_urls.
- If Linear is available and an issue ID is known, post a review-summary comment.
- If Linear is unavailable, include the review summary in the final response instead.

### 1. Restate the issue

Start with one sentence:

> This branch closes <issue> by delivering <summary>.

If no issue ID is known, derive the summary from the branch name and commit subjects.

### 2. Verify green state

Run the repo's required tests before creating any PRs.

If tests fail, stop. Report the failing command and relevant output. Do not submit PRs.

### 3. Determine slices

If .drain-handoff.json exists, read its slices array.

If it does not exist, derive slices from git:

`git log main..HEAD --reverse --format=%H%x00%B%x00%x00`

Use each commit as one slice. The first line of the commit message is the title; the remaining body is the Why.

If multiple unrelated changes are squashed into one commit, warn that the branch was not sliced during build. Continue only if it appears to be a single logical change, or the operator explicitly accepts the risk.

### 4. Choose submission path

Check Graphite preconditions in order:

`which gt gt auth status gt repo init --check`

Use these rules:

- If all checks pass, use the Graphite path.
- If gt is absent, use the plain-git path.
- If gt exists but auth or repo initialization fails, stop and show the exact error. Do not silently fall back to git.

### 5. Graphite path

For each slice, oldest first:

- Create or track a Graphite branch at that exact commit.
- Ensure the branch points to the slice commit, not the stack tip.
- Draft the PR body using the template below.

Submit the stack:

`gt stack submit --no-interactive`

After submission, set or correct each PR body with gh pr edit.

Wrap each gt call in a bounded recovery loop:

- On success: continue.
- On stale parent or out-of-sync error: run gt repo sync, retry once.
- On dirty working tree: stash, retry once, then restore the stash.
- On auth or repo initialization error: stop immediately.
- On any second failure: stop and report the exact error.

### 6. Plain-git path

Use this only when gt is absent.

For each slice, oldest first:

- Create a branch from the slice parent named <issue-or-branch>/<slug>-<n>.
- Cherry-pick the slice commit.
- Create the PR using the shared body template.
- Push the branch and create the PR with gh pr create.

The plain-git path must produce the same PR body and final trail as the Graphite path.

### 7. Verify PRs

Before posting any final trail, verify every PR URL is reachable:

`gh pr view <url>`

If any PR is missing or unreachable, stop and report the problem. Do not post the Linear comment yet.

If .drain-handoff.json exists, update it with pr_urls:

`{ "pr_urls": [ { "title": "<PR title>", "url": "<https://...>" } ] }`

### 8. Post final trail

If Linear is available and an issue ID is known, post one review-summary comment to the issue.

If Linear is unavailable or no issue ID is known, include the same summary in the final response.

If repo policy does not permit PRs, follow the repo policy and note the deviation in the summary.

## PR body template

Use this exact structure for every PR:

`## What <One to three sentences describing what this slice changes. Keep this diff-level, not ticket-level.> ## Why <Why this slice exists, what issue it supports, and why it can be reviewed independently. For later PRs, reference the prior PR when useful.> ## Focus <Specific files, decisions, or patterns to review. If there are no critical areas, write: "No critical review areas - safe for auto-merge.">`

## Review-summary template

`PR stack submitted: <n> PR(s) <slice-1-title>: <url> <slice-2-title>: <url> Path: graphite | plain-git Pre-submission review findings: critical: N, required: N (fixed: N, deferred: N)`

## Exit criteria

The skill is complete only when:

1. Tests passed before PR creation.
2. Each slice has exactly one PR.
3. Each PR body contains What, Why, and Focus.
4. Every PR URL was verified reachable.
5. Existing .drain-handoff.json, if present, was updated with pr_urls.
6. Graphite precondition failures halted instead of falling back silently.
7. The final review summary was posted to Linear when available, or reported to the operator when Linear is unavailable.
```

## ABA-391 Spec — `base` param for `/shape:pr-finishing`

### The surface
This skill is **instructions-as-code** (`skills/pr-finishing/SKILL.md`, 5.8K). "Implementing" means editing prose the worker LLM follows, not Python. The literal `main` appears in exactly **one** command (line 71), but the base concept threads through three places: slicing (§3), the Graphite bottom-PR target (§5), and the plain-git bottom-PR target (§6).

### What changes

**1. New `Inputs` line + an `Arguments`/resolution note**
Add an optional base branch input, default `main`. Resolution order: explicit skill argument → `.drain-handoff.json` `base` key (if present) → `main`. Byte-for-byte unchanged behaviour when unset.

> The drain prompt passes it as free-text ("pass `<base>` to the skill as its base branch"), so the skill must accept it from the **invocation argument**, not only the handoff file. State both sources explicitly.

**2. §3 Determine slices** — replace the hardcoded range:
```
git log main..HEAD …   →   git log <base>..HEAD …
```
with a sentence: "Let `<base>` be the resolved base branch (default `main`). Slice `<base>..HEAD`."

**3. §5 Graphite path** — the stack already tracks branches at each commit, so `gt stack submit` targets the correct parent automatically **once the branches are cut from `<base>`-rooted commits**. The one addition: the bottom branch of the stack must be parented on `<base>`. Add: "Track the oldest slice's Graphite branch with `<base>` as its parent (`gt track --parent <base>` / equivalent), so the bottom PR targets `<base>`, not `main`."

**4. §6 Plain-git path** — already "branch from the slice parent" (correct by construction), but the bottom PR's `gh pr create` defaults `--base main`. Add: "The bottom PR targets `<base>`: pass `--base <base>` on the oldest slice's `gh pr create`. Later slices target the prior slice's branch as today."

**5. Acceptance check (already in the issue)** — a chained two-issue stack produces two PRs with non-overlapping diffs. Add a manual verification note since the skill has no automated test harness.

### Scope boundary (Not Doing)
- **No change to `main` as default** — every non-chained caller keeps working untouched.
- **No drain-cycle changes** — that half shipped. This is purely the skill repo.
- **No new handoff schema field required** — `base` arrives via the invocation argument; reading it from the handoff file is a nice-to-have second source, not required for correctness (the drain prompt already passes it inline).

### Risk
The only subtle bit is §5: Graphite's `gt track --parent` must point the stack bottom at `<base>`. If that's wrong, `gt stack submit` will still target `main` and re-include lower commits — the exact bug this closes. That's the line to test by eye on a real two-issue chain.

---

This is small (one file, ~4 prose edits) and the Linear issue's What/Why/Acceptance already covers the rest. Want me to **append this implementation spec to ABA-391's description** as a comment, or save it as a doc in the shaper repo — or just start building it there?

130	If any PR is missing or unreachable, stop and report the problem. Do not post the Linear comment yet.
131	
132	If .drain-handoff.json exists, update it with pr_urls:
133	
134	`{ "pr_urls": [ { "title": "<PR title>", "url": "<https://...>" } ] }`
135	
136	### 8. Post final trail
137	
138	If Linear is available and an issue ID is known, post one review-summary comment to the issue.
139	
140	If Linear is unavailable or no issue ID is known, include the same summary in the final response.
141	
142	If repo policy does not permit PRs, follow the repo policy and note the deviation in the summary.
143	
144	## PR body template
145	
146	Use this exact structure for every PR:
147	
148	`## What <One to three sentences describing what this slice changes. Keep this diff-level, not ticket-level.> ## Why <Why this slice exists, what issue it supports, and why it can be reviewed independently. For later PRs, reference the prior PR when useful.> ## Focus <Specific files, decisions, or patterns to review. If there are no critical areas, write: "No critical review areas - safe for auto-merge.">`
149	
150	## Review-summary template
151	
152	`PR stack submitted: <n> PR(s) <slice-1-title>: <url> <slice-2-title>: <url> Path: graphite | plain-git Pre-submission review findings: critical: N, required: N (fixed: N, deferred: N)`
153	
154	## Exit criteria
155	
156	The skill is complete only when:
157	
158	1. Tests passed before PR creation.
159	2. Each slice has exactly one PR.
160	3. Each PR body contains What, Why, and Focus.
161	4. Every PR URL was verified reachable.
162	5. Existing .drain-handoff.json, if present, was updated with pr_urls.
163	6. Graphite precondition failures halted instead of falling back silently.
164	7. The final review summary was posted to Linear when available, or reported to the operator when Linear is unavailable.
165	
```

A Markdown-only skill pack — no build system, no tests, no CI. Every artefact is a `.md` file. The deliverable is well-authored prose, not runnable code.

See `CLAUDE.md` for repo structure, authoring rules (skills, hooks), and commit style.

--
- This repo pushes directly to **main**; open a PR only when the owner asks. An issue is not Done until the work is committed and pushed — local-only work doesn't count.
- Update issue status at the moment of state change, not batched at session end. Blocked work stays In Progress with a comment naming the blocker.
- Shape a new initiative with the `initiative-shape` skill; don't hand-create goal-directed projects. Initiative-shaped work (5+ issues, clear goal) slots into the *next* cycle; a bug or one-off (< 5 issues) goes on the backlog and into the ops slot if urgent.
- If an issue names a delegate (`Delegates to` / `On pickup`), invoke that skill to break the node into build tasks before coding (ktlo issues carry no breakdown step).

## Git
--
Conventional-commit-ish prefixes: `feat:`, `fix:`, `chore:`, `docs:`, `refactor:`. Subject line ≤ 70 chars; details in the body if needed. Do not add `Co-Authored-By` trailers.

Until the skill honours that base, a chained issue's PR slices `main..HEAD` and therefore re-includes every commit from the issues beneath it in the stack — the PR diff is wrong and un-reviewable.\n\nThis is the cross-repo half of drain-cycle Increment 2 (cross-issue Graphite chaining). The drain-cycle side (per-repo base baton, chain-halt on missing `pr_urls`, base threaded into the prompt) is already shipped to `drain-cycle` main.\n\n## Acceptance\n\n* The skill accepts a base branch (default `main`, preserving current behaviour byte-for-byte when unset).\n* With a non-`main` base it slices `<base>..HEAD` and the bottom PR of the stack targets `<base>`.\n* The worker invocation contract matches what the drain-cycle stack prompt passes (see `_stack_preamble` in `drain_cycle/prompt.py`: the prompt instructs the worker to \"pass `<base>` to the skill as its base branch\").\n* A chained two-issue stack produces two PRs whose diffs don't overlap.","priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-391/teach-shapepr-finishing-a-base-param-so-it-slices-basehead","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-391-teach-shapepr-finishing-a-base-param-so-it-slices-basehead","createdAt":"2026-06-11T12:49:14.062Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-11T13:12:46.767Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":"2026-06-11T13:12:46.742Z","startedAt":"2026-06-11T13:10:45.599Z","canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Done","statusType":"completed","labels":["agent-skills-shaper"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills","projectId":"f9399232-74f6-45fe-977c-dde359f2c035","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","cycleId":"793fc9ea-08c7-40c1-8c1b-417efb20adbf"}

Done. **ABA-391 shipped and closed.**

`/shape:pr-finishing` in `agent-skills-shaper` (`586501b`, on main) now honours an optional `<base>`:
- §3 slices `git log <base>..HEAD` (default `main`)
- §5 Graphite parents the bottom slice on `<base>` via `gt track --parent`
- §6 plain-git passes `--base <base>` on the bottom PR

Byte-unchanged for every non-chained caller. As a bonus, the old `gh pr create` line had a stray non-breaking space — normalized in the same edit.

This closes the cross-repo half of drain-cycle Increment 2. Both halves now line up: the orchestrator chains worktrees off the prior issue's branch and threads the base into the worker prompt, and the skill it invokes finally slices and targets that base instead of re-including the whole stack. Let me update the memory note to reflect the full feature landing.
